47/// Whether record/replay verification hashes syscall output buffers, and
48/// therefore whether its log comparison can see buffer CONTENT at all.
49///
50/// ONE CONSTANT BECAUSE TWO INDEPENDENT SWITCHES HAD TO AGREE AND SILENTLY DID
51/// NOT MATTER. Record/replay carried this decision twice: `metadata.rs`'s
52/// `record_or_replay_config` decided whether the recorder EMITS the hash, and
53/// `record_start.rs`'s `ComparisonOptions` decided whether the comparator READS
54/// it. Both were hard-coded `false`, neither was reachable from the command
55/// line, and nothing related them. Setting either alone achieves nothing: hash
56/// without compare is dead weight, compare without hash finds nothing. Naming
57/// the decision once makes the pair impossible to desynchronize, and makes
58/// changing it a one-line edit in a place a reader can find.
59pub const RECORD_REPLAY_HASHES_IO_BUFFERS: bool = true;
60
61/// Whether record/replay virtualizes time. FALSE, DELIBERATELY, AND THAT IS WHY
62/// A GREEN RECORD/REPLAY VERDICT IS NOT A DETERMINISM RESULT.
63///
64/// Named for the same reason as [`RECORD_REPLAY_HASHES_IO_BUFFERS`] directly
65/// above: the decision was about to be carried twice. `metadata.rs`'s
66/// `record_or_replay_config` sets the config the recorder and replayer run
67/// under, and `record_start.rs`'s `ComparisonOptions` now discloses that setting
68/// in the verdict. If those two disagreed, the report would describe a time
69/// policy the run did not use — which is precisely the class of defect the
70/// disclosure exists to close, reintroduced one layer up.
71///
72/// `hermit run --verify` reads its value from the live `det_config` instead,
73/// because `--no-virtualize-time` makes it a genuine runtime choice there. Only
74/// the record/replay path is a fixed decision, so only it gets a constant.
75pub const RECORD_REPLAY_VIRTUALIZES_TIME: bool = false;
76
77/// Exit status for a completed comparison that found a divergence.
78///
79/// This is the conventional `diff` result: zero means equal and one means
80/// different. The verification JSON distinguishes it from a guest that exits
81/// one of its own accord.
82pub const HERMIT_VERIFICATION_DIVERGENCE_EXIT: i32 = 1;
83
84/// Exit status for a failure of HERMIT ITSELF, as distinct from the guest's.
85///
86/// ⚠️ EVERY CLI ERROR USED TO BE `Exited(1)`, WHICH IS THE SINGLE MOST COMMON
87/// GUEST FAILURE CODE. A tracer panic and a guest that exited 1 of its own
88/// accord were therefore INDISTINGUISHABLE from `$?` alone — and every harness,
89/// gate and script on this project decides pass/fail from exactly that value.
90/// The information was not missing, only discarded: reverie's container reports
91/// the child's real status as a typed `ExitStatus`, and hermit collapsed it to
92/// prose and then to `1`.
93///
94/// WHY THIS DOES NOT BREAK THE "hermit's exit IS the guest's exit" CONTRACT.
95/// That contract describes the case where the guest RAN AND EXITED, and it is
96/// untouched: a guest status still flows through `raise_or_exit` unchanged, and
97/// only the `Err` arm — where there is NO guest exit code, because hermit failed
98/// before or instead of producing one — uses this value. The contract did not
99/// cover that case; it does now.
100///
101/// WHY 125. It is the established convention for "the wrapper tool itself
102/// failed" — GNU `env`, `chroot` and `timeout` all reserve 125 for exactly this,
103/// leaving 126/127 for exec-level problems. It is not a value hermit emits
104/// anywhere else.
105///
106/// ⚠️ THIS REDUCES A COLLISION, IT DOES NOT REMOVE ONE. A guest is free to exit
107/// 125 deliberately, and every value in 0..=255 is a legal guest status, so no
108/// code can be reserved outright. What it removes is the GUARANTEED collision
109/// with the most common failure code. For an unambiguous signal, read the
110/// `HERMIT_TASK_PANIC` marker on stderr or the verification JSON; this makes the
111/// cheap check — `$?` — stop actively lying.
112///
113/// ⚠️ IT LIVES IN THE LIBRARY, NOT IN `main.rs`, AND THAT PLACEMENT IS THE FIX
114/// FOR HOW IT WENT STALE. It was a private `const` inside the binary, so
115/// `tests/cli.rs` — the only thing that asserts on it — could not name it and
116/// wrote the number out by hand instead. When hermit#2558 moved the value from
117/// `1` to `125`, the definition moved and the sixteen copies did not: eight cli
118/// tests went red on main and stayed red, each failing an assertion about the
119/// exit code rather than about the behaviour it exists to check. Same shape as
120/// [`RECORD_REPLAY_HASHES_IO_BUFFERS`] above — a decision carried in two places
121/// that had to agree and had no way to. Exported here, the tests move with it.
122///
123/// ⚠️ DO NOT FLIP EVERY `Some(1)` YOU FIND. `tests/stress_suite.rs`
124/// asserts `Some(1)` as THE GUEST'S OWN SIGNAL, not as a stale copy of this
125/// constant: there `Some(1) => GuestOutcome::Exposed`, and
126/// `status.code() == Some(1)` IS the `exposes_bug` predicate. It is
127/// pattern-identical to the tests that DID go stale, and it is the OPPOSITE
128/// case -- the assertion is CORRECT and must not move with this constant.
129///
130/// ⚠️ ITS FAILURE MODE IS SILENT SUCCESS. Changing it to 125 would not
131/// turn the stress suite red; it would make `exposes_bug` NEVER FIRE, so the
132/// suite would report no bug exposed forever and pass while blind. A red is
133/// recoverable; a green that cannot fail is not.
134///
135/// REVIEWER RULE: a change to `tests/stress_suite.rs` inside an exit-code head
136/// SHOULD BE REFUSED ON SIGHT AND QUESTIONED.
137pub const HERMIT_INTERNAL_FAILURE_EXIT: i32 = 125;
138
139/// A deadline hermit itself was asked to enforce expired: **124**.
140///
141/// The status the container init exits with when a hermit-owned bound ends the
142/// run -- `run --timeout`'s SIGALRM fallback here, and `record --record-timeout`'s
143/// handler in `record_start.rs`, which has always used this value. 124 is the
144/// established code for "a deadline fired" across GNU `timeout`, `safehermit`'s
145/// wall bound and both hermit spellings, so this names an existing convention
146/// rather than claiming a new number.
147///
148/// ⚠️ IT MUST BE READ AT THE CONTAINER BOUNDARY OR IT IS SILENTLY REWRITTEN TO
149/// 125. Found only by forcing the fallback to fire, 2026-08-26: the init exited
150/// 124, `classify_container_result` had no arm for it, and it fell through to
151/// `ContainerChildExit` -- so the run reported `exit 125` and
152/// `class=container-child-exit`, "the child died with a status it did not
153/// pick", for a deadline hermit chose to enforce. Identical in shape to the
154/// refusal that arrives as 122 and needs its own arm for the same reason.
155pub const HERMIT_DEADLINE_EXIT: i32 = 124;
156
157/// Hermit's own log output exceeded `--max-log-bytes`: **123**.
158///
159/// The status hermit exits with when the byte budget on its tracing output
160/// (stderr, or `--log-file`) is exhausted and it deliberately tears the run
161/// down. 123 was already reserved below for "a LOG BYTE CAP fired" on behalf of
162/// dev-hermit's `bin/safehermit`; this is the same event enforced one layer
163/// lower, exactly as 124 is shared by hermit's `--timeout` and safehermit's wall
164/// deadline. It is not 125 because the cap working is not hermit breaking, and
165/// not 122 because no fail-closed determinism policy was involved: the remedy
166/// is "lower the log level or raise the cap", not "change the program".
167///
168/// Like 124, it must be read at the container boundary
169/// (`classify_container_result`) or it is rewritten to 125.
170pub const HERMIT_LOG_CAP_EXIT: i32 = 123;
171
172/// The guest program could not be found at all: **127**.
173///
174/// Exported for the same reason as [`HERMIT_INTERNAL_FAILURE_EXIT`] and with the
175/// same history one step behind it. `GuestProgramFault::exit_code` wrote `127`
176/// and `126` as bare literals, and `tests/cli.rs` had no way to name either, so
177/// a test meaning "not found" and a test meaning "hermit refused" were asserted
178/// with the same helper and the same number until the values diverged.
179///
180/// ⚠️ THIS IS NOT A SUBDIVISION OF HERMIT'S OWN FAILURE, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE
181/// DISTINCTION. 125 says *hermit* broke; 127 and 126 say *the guest program* was
182/// unusable and hermit correctly declined to start it. A caller that cannot tell
183/// those apart cannot tell "fix my tooling" from "fix my command line".
184pub const GUEST_PROGRAM_NOT_FOUND_EXIT: i32 = 127;
185
186/// The guest program exists but cannot be executed as given: **126**.
187///
188/// See [`GUEST_PROGRAM_NOT_FOUND_EXIT`]. 127/126 are the GNU `env`/`chroot`/
189/// `timeout` convention that 125 itself came from, so the three sit in one
190/// scheme rather than being three unrelated numbers.
191pub const GUEST_PROGRAM_NOT_EXECUTABLE_EXIT: i32 = 126;
192
193// ⚠️ THE VALUES THEMSELVES ARE PINNED, NOT ONLY THEIR NAMES.
194//
195// Naming a constant makes every consumer agree with the definition; it does not
196// make the definition right. Once `tests/cli.rs` asserts `Some(THE_CONSTANT)`
197// everywhere, the whole suite is self-consistent and completely unanchored -- a
198// one-character edit here would move all sixteen assertions with it and nothing
199// would fail. That is the exact shape of the defect this scheme exists to close,
200// displaced one level up, and it is why these are compile-time assertions rather
201// than a comment: a deliberate edit "would be noticed" is the argument that
202// already failed once, when hermit#2558 moved 1 -> 125 and sixteen copies went
203// stale.
204//
205// WHY EACH VALUE AND NOT ANOTHER. GNU `env`, `chroot` and `timeout` reserve 125
206// for "the wrapper itself failed", 127 for "command not found" and 126 for
207// "found but not executable". Borrowing the whole scheme rather than one number
208// is what keeps the three answers distinguishable from each other.
209//
210// ⚠️ THE EXIT-CODE ALLOCATION FOR THE `hermit` BINARY. ONE TABLE, HERE.
211//
212// Five separate exit-code defects were found in one evening and every one of
213// them came from a value being chosen, copied or rewritten somewhere that could
214// not see the others. This is the single place the space is allocated; anything
215// that emits or rewrites a hermit exit status is a consumer OF this table and
216// must not invent its own reading of it.
217//
218//   0          success.
219//   1          HERMIT_VERIFICATION_DIVERGENCE_EXIT, matching `diff`; also a
220//              legal guest status. Read the verification JSON to distinguish.
221//   2 ..= 121  THE GUEST'S OWN STATUS, passed through untouched. (The range
222//              stops at 121 because 122 is reserved below; a guest may still
223//              CHOOSE any value, see the caveat at the end.)
224//   122        HERMIT_POLICY_REFUSAL_EXIT -- hermit REFUSED the run under a
225//              fail-closed policy. Hermit WORKED; the run was stopped on
226//              purpose and the reason was printed. Distinct from 125 because
227//              "hermit refused" and "hermit broke" demand opposite responses:
228//              read the refusal and change your program or flags, versus file a
229//              bug. Defined in `detcore-model` because `detcore` emits it and
230//              this crate recognises it, and that is the only crate both
231//              depend on.
232//   123        HERMIT_LOG_CAP_EXIT -- a LOG BYTE CAP fired. Hermit's own
233//              `--max-log-bytes` budget on its tracing output, and the value
234//              reserved for dev-hermit's `bin/safehermit` log-cap kill (which,
235//              as of 2026-09-26, still exits 125 -- see the 125 note below).
236//              Hermit's spelling prints `HERMIT_LOG_CAP class=log-cap`.
237//   124        DO NOT USE. GNU `timeout`'s deadline, and dev-hermit's
238//              `bin/safehermit` WALL DEADLINE kill. `tests/cli.rs` asserts
239//              `assert_ne!(code, Some(124))` on the awk-mincore probe.
240//   125        HERMIT_INTERNAL_FAILURE_EXIT -- hermit itself failed, no guest
241//              ran. NOT YET THE SOLE MEANING through dev-hermit's
242//              `bin/safehermit`: that wrapper has exited 125 for its
243//              log-byte-cap kill since it was created on 2026-08-20 and still
244//              did on 2026-10-05, so a run through it produces one number for
245//              two faults with opposite remedies (see "125 ⚠️ NOT FREE" below).
246//              An earlier version of this table said safehermit had already
247//              moved off 125; it never did. The intended fix is that SAFEHERMIT
248//              MOVES -- to the 123 reserved for it above -- NOT HERMIT, because
249//              that wrapper is the only layer that knows which happened (it
250//              relays hermit's status otherwise) and because 125/126/127 is one
251//              GNU scheme that cannot be broken up. Tracked as
252//              `hermit_s_125_collides`.
253//   126        GUEST_PROGRAM_NOT_EXECUTABLE_EXIT.
254//   127        GUEST_PROGRAM_NOT_FOUND_EXIT -- the ABSOLUTE-PATH form. The
255//              bare-name-on-guest-PATH form currently exits 125 instead, which
256//              is an inconsistency tracked as `hermit_reports_a_missing`, not a
257//              second meaning for 127.
258//   128 + N    killed by signal N (shell convention). Hermit does not emit these
259//              deliberately; a wrapper reporting a signal death must use them
260//              rather than borrow a code that already means something.
261//
262// ⚠️ NO CODE IS EXCLUSIVELY HERMIT'S. Every value in 0..=255 is a legal guest
263// status, so a guest may return 125 or 127 of its own accord and this table
264// cannot stop it. The table removes GUARANTEED collisions, not possible ones.
265// The only unambiguous discriminator is the `HERMIT_INTERNAL_FAILURE` marker on
266// stderr, present exactly when hermit itself failed. Any consumer deciding on
267// `$?` alone is guessing, and should say so.
268//
269// ⚠️ THE SAME ARGUMENT APPLIES TO 124, AND MORE SHARPLY, BECAUSE FIVE
270// MECHANISMS PRODUCE IT. `hermit run --timeout`, its unwind fallback, `hermit
271// record --record-timeout`, the `timeout(1)` wrapped around a manifest cell, and
272// `safehermit`'s wall deadline all exit 124, and GNU `timeout` uses it for the
273// same event. They demand different responses -- a slow guest, a wedged
274// teardown inside hermit, a cell bound set too low, a run that escaped every
275// inner bound -- so `$?` cannot route the reader to any of them.
276//
277// The discriminator is again the marker on stderr, and it is a CONTRACT rather
278// than a diagnostic nicety: `class=run-timeout` for hermit's own bound (which
279// also states the bound in seconds), `HERMIT_RUN_TIMEOUT_FALLBACK` for the
280// unwind failing to complete, `safehermit: bound.wall=` for the cgroup reap.
281// EXIT 124 WITH NO MARKER AT ALL MEANS NO INNER BOUND FIRED -- something outside
282// hermit killed the run -- which is a configuration error and not a slow guest.
283// The full picture, including which rung bounds which quantity and the
284// strict-inequality invariant between them, is docs/TIMEOUT_LADDER.md.
285//
286// ⚠️ DO NOT CLAMP INTO THIS RANGE. `scripts/hermit-code-coverage.rs` used
287// `clamp(1, 125)` and so rewrote 127 and 126 into 125 -- turning "your program is
288// missing" into "hermit broke" and pointing the reader at the wrong project.
289// Truncating a status does not lose information here, it MANUFACTURES a false
290// attribution, because the ceiling is itself a meaning.
291//
292// ⚠️ WHAT EACH VALUE MUST NOT COLLIDE WITH -- AND 125 ALREADY DOES.
293//
294//   0    success. Never a failure code; see the nonzero pin below.
295//   1    the commonest guest exit status. Sharing it is the collision
296//        hermit#2558 introduced 125 to escape, so 125 must never drift back.
297//   124  GNU `timeout`'s "deadline fired", and dev-hermit's `bin/safehermit`
298//        uses it for its WALL DEADLINE kill. `tests/cli.rs` asserts
299//        `assert_ne!(code, Some(124))` on the awk-mincore probe for that reason.
300//   126  hermit's own GUEST_PROGRAM_NOT_EXECUTABLE_EXIT.
301//   127  hermit's own GUEST_PROGRAM_NOT_FOUND_EXIT -- already in use for the
302//        ABSOLUTE-PATH form of a missing guest program. Note the bare-name form
303//        resolved against the guest PATH exits 125 instead, which is an
304//        inconsistency filed separately, not a licence to reuse 127.
305//
306//   125  ⚠️ NOT FREE. dev-hermit's `bin/safehermit` exits 125 to mean "the LOG
307//        BYTE CAP fired: safehermit killed the run through its cgroup"
308//        (`demos/lib/demo_common.py`'s SAFEHERMIT_EXIT_REASON). Any hermit run
309//        launched through that wrapper -- which is how the demos and the repeat
310//        harness run it -- can therefore produce 125 for two unrelated reasons:
311//        hermit refused, or the wrapper killed it for log volume. They demand
312//        opposite responses (fix the invocation vs raise the cap), and `$?`
313//        cannot tell them apart. This is a LIVE ambiguity, not a hypothetical:
314//        demo 5 has been observed reporting `exited with status 125` when the
315//        cap fired. It is recorded here rather than resolved because changing
316//        either value is a cross-repository decision.
317//
318//        The discriminator meanwhile is the same one that separates hermit's 125
319//        from a guest's own 125: the `HERMIT_INTERNAL_FAILURE` marker on stderr,
320//        present only when hermit itself failed. safehermit writes its verdict
321//        to its `--sh-report` file instead. Read one of those, never the number
322//        alone.
323//
324// ⚠️ 0 IS THE DANGEROUS DRIFT, NOT 126 OR 127. Every one of these is asserted as
325// `assert_eq!(code, Some(CONST))`. At 0 those assertions would stop demanding a
326// FAILURE and start demanding a SUCCESS, and would still pass -- sixteen tests
327// silently inverted. `tests/cli.rs` also asserts `!status.success()` beside the
328// code for that reason; this pin is the second of the two locks.
329const _: () = assert!(
330    HERMIT_VERIFICATION_DIVERGENCE_EXIT == 1,
331    "1 is the established diff-style result for a completed comparison that diverged"
332);
333const _: () = assert!(
334    HERMIT_INTERNAL_FAILURE_EXIT == 125,
335    "125 is the GNU wrapper-failed convention; 1 is the commonest guest status and 0 is success"
336);
337const _: () = assert!(
338    GUEST_PROGRAM_NOT_FOUND_EXIT == 127,
339    "127 is the GNU command-not-found convention and must stay distinct from 125 and 126"
340);
341const _: () = assert!(
342    GUEST_PROGRAM_NOT_EXECUTABLE_EXIT == 126,
343    "126 is the GNU found-but-not-executable convention and must stay distinct from 125 and 127"
344);
345const _: () = assert!(
346    HERMIT_LOG_CAP_EXIT == 123,
347    "123 is Hermit's log-cap code, reserved for bin/safehermit's log-cap kill as well; see the allocation table"
348);
349const _: () = assert!(
350    HERMIT_LOG_CAP_EXIT != 0
351        && HERMIT_VERIFICATION_DIVERGENCE_EXIT != 0
352        && HERMIT_INTERNAL_FAILURE_EXIT != 0
353        && GUEST_PROGRAM_NOT_FOUND_EXIT != 0
354        && GUEST_PROGRAM_NOT_EXECUTABLE_EXIT != 0,
355    "a failure code of 0 would invert every assert_eq!(code, Some(CONST)) into demanding success"
356);

448fn read_iovecs<M: reverie::syscalls::MemoryAccess>(
449    memory: &M,
450    message: &libc::msghdr,
451) -> Result<Vec<libc::iovec>, reverie::Errno> {
452    if message.msg_iovlen == 0 {
453        return Ok(Vec::new());
454    }
455    let address = reverie::syscalls::Addr::from_raw(message.msg_iov as usize)
456        .ok_or(reverie::Errno::EFAULT)?;
457    let mut iovecs = vec![
458        libc::iovec {
459            iov_base: std::ptr::null_mut(),
460            iov_len: 0,
461        };
462        message.msg_iovlen
463    ];
464    memory.read_values(address, &mut iovecs)?;
465    Ok(iovecs)
466}
467
468fn vectored_offset(low: u64, high: u64) -> i64 {
469    if std::mem::size_of::<usize>() == 8 {
470        low as i64
471    } else {
472        ((high << 32) | (low & u32::MAX as u64)) as i64
473    }
474}
475
476enum KvmStdinReservation {
477    Open(fs::File),
478    Closed,
479}
480
481static KVM_STDIN_RESERVATION: Mutex<Option<KvmStdinReservation>> = Mutex::new(None);
482
483/// Saves stdin captured before Rust's process startup can reuse a closed fd 0.
484pub fn reserve_kvm_stdin(stdin: Option<fs::File>) -> io::Result<()> {
485    let mut reservation = KVM_STDIN_RESERVATION
486        .lock()
487        .map_err(|_| io::Error::other("KVM stdin reservation lock is poisoned"))?;
488    if reservation.is_some() {
489        return Err(io::Error::new(
490            io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists,
491            "KVM stdin is already reserved",
492        ));
493    }
494    *reservation = Some(match stdin {
495        Some(file) => KvmStdinReservation::Open(file),
496        None => KvmStdinReservation::Closed,
497    });
498    Ok(())
499}
500
501fn duplicate_current_stdin() -> io::Result<Option<fs::File>> {
502    // SAFETY: F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC duplicates fd 0 without taking ownership of it.
503    let duplicate = unsafe { libc::fcntl(libc::STDIN_FILENO, libc::F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC, 3) };
504    if duplicate >= 0 {
505        // SAFETY: F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC returned a new owned descriptor.
506        return Ok(Some(unsafe { fs::File::from_raw_fd(duplicate) }));
507    }
508    let error = io::Error::last_os_error();
509    if error.raw_os_error() == Some(libc::EBADF) {
510        Ok(None)
511    } else {
512        Err(error)
513    }
514}
515
516fn ensure_kvm_stdin_reserved() -> io::Result<()> {
517    let mut reservation = KVM_STDIN_RESERVATION
518        .lock()
519        .map_err(|_| io::Error::other("KVM stdin reservation lock is poisoned"))?;
520    if reservation.is_none() {
521        *reservation = Some(match duplicate_current_stdin()? {
522            Some(file) => KvmStdinReservation::Open(file),
523            None => KvmStdinReservation::Closed,
524        });
525    }
526    Ok(())
527}
528
529fn reserved_kvm_stdin() -> Result<Option<fs::File>, Error> {
530    ensure_kvm_stdin_reserved()?;
531    let reservation = KVM_STDIN_RESERVATION
532        .lock()
533        .map_err(|_| io::Error::other("KVM stdin reservation lock is poisoned"))?;
534    match reservation.as_ref() {
535        Some(KvmStdinReservation::Open(file)) => Ok(Some(file.try_clone()?)),
536        Some(KvmStdinReservation::Closed) => Ok(None),
537        None => unreachable!("stdin reservation was initialized above"),
538    }
539}
540
541/// A replayable snapshot of the guest's stdin for the output-capturing backends.
542///
543/// `hermit run --verify` executes the guest twice and compares the two runs. The
544/// output-capturing backends historically fed the guest `Stdio::null()`, so any
545/// data piped into hermit (`echo prog | hermit run --strict --verify -- ...`)
546/// was silently dropped. Both runs then saw identical *empty* input and hermit
547/// reported a false "deterministic" success even though the guest never received
548/// its input. `Seekable` holds a read-only, rewindable file so both runs receive
549/// the exact same bytes without being able to modify the caller's input; `None`
550/// means stdin should be `/dev/null` (nothing to replay, or a terminal that
551/// cannot be replayed identically to two runs).
552enum StdinSnapshot {
553    Seekable(fs::File),
554    None,
555}
556
557static OUTPUT_STDIN_SNAPSHOT: Mutex<Option<StdinSnapshot>> = Mutex::new(None);
558
559/// Records a rewindable snapshot of the process stdin so the output-capturing
560/// backends can replay identical input to each run of `hermit run --verify`.
561///
562/// `stdin` is the descriptor captured before Rust startup could reuse a closed
563/// fd 0 (see the binary's `startup_stdin`). A regular-file redirect is reopened
564/// read-only; a pipe/fifo/socket is drained once into a seekable temporary file
565/// and that file is reopened read-only. A terminal (or absent stdin) is treated
566/// as `/dev/null` because a live terminal cannot be replayed identically to two
567/// runs.
568pub fn reserve_output_stdin_snapshot(stdin: Option<fs::File>) -> io::Result<()> {
569    let snapshot = match stdin {
570        None => StdinSnapshot::None,
571        Some(mut file) => {
572            // SAFETY: as_raw_fd borrows the descriptor without taking ownership.
573            let is_tty = unsafe { libc::isatty(file.as_raw_fd()) } == 1;
574            if is_tty {
575                StdinSnapshot::None
576            } else {
577                let replay = if file.stream_position().is_ok() {
578                    // Already seekable (e.g. `< file`): retain it below through
579                    // a read-only descriptor with an independent file offset.
580                    file
581                } else {
582                    // Non-seekable (pipe/fifo/socket): buffer once into a
583                    // seekable temporary file so both --verify runs receive
584                    // identical input.
585                    //
586                    // NAME THE WAIT BEFORE ENTERING IT. This `io::copy` reads to
587                    // EOF, so a stdin that never reaches EOF -- an inherited socket,
588                    // a fifo whose writer stays open -- blocks here FOREVER, before
589                    // the guest has started. Measured: the process wedges with ZERO
590                    // bytes on stdout and stderr and is only cleared by killing it,
591                    // which is indistinguishable from a slow run. That
592                    // indistinguishability is the whole harm; an unbounded wait that
593                    // says what it is waiting for is merely slow, and a reader can
594                    // act on it.
595                    //
596                    // Deliberately UNCONDITIONAL rather than emitted after a delay:
597                    // a message that appears only once N seconds have passed makes
598                    // hermit's own stderr depend on timing, and this project does
599                    // not accept timing-dependent output. Deliberately NOT a
600                    // deadline either -- a 12s slow producer is legitimate and
601                    // delivers, and nothing can separate "slow" from "never" except
602                    // by waiting. This changes no control flow: every input that
603                    // worked before still works, byte for byte, on stdout.
604                    const BUFFERING_NOTE: &str = "hermit: --verify is buffering stdin from a non-seekable stream so both \
605                         runs receive identical input. If this appears to hang, stdin has not \
606                         reached end-of-file; pass `< /dev/null` when the guest needs no input.";
607                    if nonwaiting_write::diagnostics_must_not_wait() {
608                        // Under --max-log-bytes, termination must not depend
609                        // on this write; see `nonwaiting_write`.
610                        nonwaiting_write::write_without_waiting(
611                            libc::STDERR_FILENO,
612                            format!("{BUFFERING_NOTE}\n").as_bytes(),
613                        );
614                    } else {
615                        eprintln!("{BUFFERING_NOTE}");
616                    }
617                    let mut buffered = tempfile::tempfile()?;
618                    io::copy(&mut file, &mut buffered)?;
619                    buffered.seek(SeekFrom::Start(0))?;
620                    buffered
621                };
622
623                // A duplicate would keep the original access mode. Reopen the
624                // reserved descriptor read-only so neither verification run
625                // can modify the caller's stdin or the next run's input.
626                let replay_path = format!("/proc/self/fd/{}", replay.as_raw_fd());
627                StdinSnapshot::Seekable(fs::File::open(replay_path)?)
628            }
629        }
630    };
631    let mut reservation = OUTPUT_STDIN_SNAPSHOT
632        .lock()
633        .map_err(|_| io::Error::other("output stdin snapshot lock is poisoned"))?;
634    if reservation.is_some() {
635        return Err(io::Error::new(
636            io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists,
637            "output stdin snapshot is already reserved",
638        ));
639    }
640    *reservation = Some(snapshot);
641    Ok(())
642}
643
644/// Returns a rewound descriptor for the reserved stdin snapshot, if any.
645///
646/// Each call rewinds the snapshot and hands back a fresh descriptor, so
647/// repeated `--verify` runs read identical input from the start. Returns `None`
648/// when no snapshot was reserved or the reserved stdin is a terminal/absent
649/// (nothing to replay). Runs are sequential (each executes in its own forked
650/// container child), so resetting the shared open file description's offset here
651/// is safe.
652fn output_backend_stdin_reservation() -> Result<(bool, Option<fs::File>), Error> {
653    let mut reservation = OUTPUT_STDIN_SNAPSHOT
654        .lock()
655        .map_err(|_| io::Error::other("output stdin snapshot lock is poisoned"))?;
656    match reservation.as_mut() {
657        Some(StdinSnapshot::Seekable(file)) => {
658            file.seek(SeekFrom::Start(0))?;
659            Ok((true, Some(file.try_clone()?)))
660        }
661        Some(StdinSnapshot::None) => Ok((true, None)),
662        None => Ok((false, None)),
663    }
664}
665
666fn output_backend_stdin_file() -> Result<Option<fs::File>, Error> {
667    Ok(output_backend_stdin_reservation()?.1)
668}
669
670/// Returns the stdin to hand a guest run in an output-capturing backend.
671///
672/// When [`reserve_output_stdin_snapshot`] has stored a replayable snapshot the
673/// file is rewound and a fresh descriptor handed to the guest, so each
674/// `--verify` run reads identical input. Otherwise (no snapshot reserved, or a
675/// terminal/absent stdin) the guest gets `/dev/null`, matching the previous
676/// behavior for callers that do not reserve a snapshot.
677fn output_backend_stdin() -> Result<Stdio, Error> {
678    match output_backend_stdin_file()? {
679        Some(file) => Ok(Stdio::from(file)),
680        None => Ok(Stdio::null()),
681    }
682}
683
684/// The result of recording a command.
685#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
686pub struct Recording {
687    /// The unique ID of the recording.
688    pub id: Id,
689
690    /// The exit code of the command.
691    pub exit_status: ExitStatus,
692}
693
694#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
695enum CapabilityProbe {
696    Namespaces,
697    Ptrace,
698    Seccomp,
699}
700
701fn run_capability_probe(probe: CapabilityProbe) -> Result<bool, Error> {
702    // SAFETY: The child calls only async-signal-safe syscalls and exits immediately.
703    let pid = unsafe { libc::fork() };
704    if pid == -1 {
705        return Err(std::io::Error::last_os_error()).context("Failed to fork capability probe");
706    }
707    if pid == 0 {
708        let supported = match probe {
709            CapabilityProbe::Namespaces => unsafe {
710                libc::unshare(libc::CLONE_NEWUSER | libc::CLONE_NEWPID) == 0
711            },
712            CapabilityProbe::Ptrace => {
713                // SAFETY: PTRACE_TRACEME ignores the pid and address arguments.
714                unsafe {
715                    libc::ptrace(
716                        libc::PTRACE_TRACEME,
717                        0,
718                        std::ptr::null_mut::<libc::c_void>(),
719                        std::ptr::null_mut::<libc::c_void>(),
720                    ) != -1
721                }
722            }
723            CapabilityProbe::Seccomp => {
724                let mut filter = libc::sock_filter {
725                    code: 0x06, // BPF_RET | BPF_K
726                    jt: 0,
727                    jf: 0,
728                    k: 0x7fff0000, // SECCOMP_RET_ALLOW
729                };
730                let program = libc::sock_fprog {
731                    len: 1,
732                    filter: &mut filter,
733                };
734                // SAFETY: The filter is an allow-all program with a valid one-element lifetime.
735                unsafe {
736                    libc::prctl(libc::PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS, 1, 0, 0, 0) == 0
737                        && libc::syscall(
738                            libc::SYS_seccomp,
739                            1, // SECCOMP_SET_MODE_FILTER
740                            0,
741                            &program,
742                        ) == 0
743                }
744            }
745        };
746        // SAFETY: Avoid running Rust destructors after fork.
747        unsafe { libc::_exit(i32::from(!supported)) }
748    }
749
750    let mut status = 0;
751    loop {
752        // SAFETY: pid is the child created above and status points to valid storage.
753        let result = unsafe { libc::waitpid(pid, &mut status, 0) };
754        if result == pid {
755            return Ok(libc::WIFEXITED(status) && libc::WEXITSTATUS(status) == 0);
756        }
757        if result == -1 {
758            let error = std::io::Error::last_os_error();
759            if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::Interrupted {
760                continue;
761            }
762            return Err(error).context("Failed to wait for capability probe");
763        }
764    }
765}
766
767fn validate_tracing_environment() -> Result<(), Error> {
768    if !run_capability_probe(CapabilityProbe::Namespaces)? {
769        anyhow::bail!(
770            "Hermit cannot create its required user and PID namespaces: \
771             unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER | CLONE_NEWPID) was denied. Allow unprivileged user namespaces \
772             and the unshare syscall in the host/container policy."
773        );
774    }
775    if !run_capability_probe(CapabilityProbe::Ptrace)? {
776        anyhow::bail!(
777            "Hermit cannot use ptrace in this environment: a child PTRACE_TRACEME probe was \
778             denied. Allow same-UID parent-child ptrace in the container seccomp and host \
779             Yama/LSM policy; CAP_SYS_PTRACE is normally not required. Use --namespace-only for \
780             a sandbox smoke test without syscall interception."
781        );
782    }
783    if !run_capability_probe(CapabilityProbe::Seccomp)? {
784        anyhow::bail!(
785            "Hermit cannot install its tracee seccomp filter: \
786             seccomp(SECCOMP_SET_MODE_FILTER) was denied. Allow seccomp and \
787             prctl(PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS) in the container policy, or use --namespace-only for a \
788             sandbox smoke test without syscall interception."
789        );
790    }
791    Ok(())
792}

794#[cfg(feature = "dbt")]
795fn is_dynamorio_sdk(path: &Path) -> bool {
796    path.join("include/dr_api.h").is_file()
797        || path.join("DynamoRIOConfig.cmake").is_file()
798        || path.join("cmake/DynamoRIOConfig.cmake").is_file()
799}

801#[cfg(feature = "dbt")]
802fn dynamorio_sdk_available() -> bool {
803    if hermit_resources::resource("dynamorio/bin64/drrun")
804        .is_ok_and(|path| path.is_some_and(|path| path.is_file()))
805    {
806        return true;
807    }
808    if reverie_dbt::bundled_drrun_path().is_file() {
809        return true;
810    }
811    const DEFAULT_ROOTS: [&str; 3] = [
812        "/usr/lib/cmake/DynamoRIO",
813        "/usr/local/lib/cmake/DynamoRIO",
814        "/opt/dynamorio",
815    ];
816
817    ["DYNAMORIO_HOME", "DynamoRIO_DIR"]
818        .into_iter()
819        .filter_map(std::env::var_os)
820        .map(PathBuf::from)
821        .chain(DEFAULT_ROOTS.into_iter().map(PathBuf::from))
822        .any(|path| is_dynamorio_sdk(&path))
823}

832    runtime.err().map(|error| {

835             cdylib in the same target directory"

839
840// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
841// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#688): Review LiteInst runtime discovery.
842/// Checks that `path` is an x86-64 shared object that exports every name in
843/// `required` and registers `initializer_name` in its constructor array, so
844/// that preloading it runs that initializer. `label` names the library in every
845/// error message. Returns the canonical path.
846fn validate_preload_constructor_library(
847    path: &Path,
848    label: &str,
849    required: &[&str],
850    initializer_name: &str,
851) -> io::Result<PathBuf> {
852    let bytes = fs::read(path).map_err(|error| {
853        io::Error::new(
854            error.kind(),
855            format!("failed to read {label} {}: {error}", path.display()),
856        )
857    })?;
858    let elf = Elf::parse(&bytes).map_err(|error| {
859        io::Error::new(
860            io::ErrorKind::InvalidData,
861            format!("{label} {} is not an ELF DSO: {error}", path.display()),
862        )
863    })?;
864    if elf.header.e_type != header::ET_DYN || elf.header.e_machine != header::EM_X86_64 {
865        return Err(io::Error::new(
866            io::ErrorKind::InvalidData,
867            format!("{label} {} is not an x86-64 shared object", path.display()),
868        ));
869    }
870
871    for &name in required {
872        if !elf
873            .dynsyms
874            .iter()
875            .any(|symbol| elf.dynstrtab.get_at(symbol.st_name) == Some(name))
876        {
877            return Err(io::Error::new(
878                io::ErrorKind::InvalidData,
879                format!(
880                    "{label} {} is missing required export {name}",
881                    path.display()
882                ),
883            ));
884        }
885    }
886    let (initializer_index, initializer) = elf
887        .dynsyms
888        .iter()
889        .enumerate()
890        .find(|(_, symbol)| elf.dynstrtab.get_at(symbol.st_name) == Some(initializer_name))
891        .ok_or_else(|| {
892            io::Error::other(format!(
893                "checked {label} initializer {initializer_name} disappeared"
894            ))
895        })?;
896    let init_array = elf
897        .section_headers
898        .iter()
899        .find(|section| section.sh_type == section_header::SHT_INIT_ARRAY)
900        .ok_or_else(|| {
901            io::Error::new(
902                io::ErrorKind::InvalidData,
903                format!("{label} {} has no constructor array", path.display()),
904            )
905        })?;
906    let init_start = init_array.sh_addr;
907    let init_end = init_start.saturating_add(init_array.sh_size);
908    let relocated_initializer = elf
909        .dynrelas
910        .iter()
911        .chain(elf.dynrels.iter())
912        .any(|relocation| {
913            (init_start..init_end).contains(&relocation.r_offset)
914                && relocation.r_sym == initializer_index
915        });
916    let init_bytes = usize::try_from(init_array.sh_offset)
917        .ok()
918        .and_then(|start| {
919            usize::try_from(init_array.sh_size)
920                .ok()
921                .and_then(|size| bytes.get(start..start.checked_add(size)?))
922        })
923        .unwrap_or_default();
924    let direct_initializer = init_bytes
925        .as_chunks::<8>()
926        .0
927        .iter()
928        .any(|entry| u64::from_le_bytes(*entry) == initializer.st_value);
929    if !relocated_initializer && !direct_initializer {
930        return Err(io::Error::new(
931            io::ErrorKind::InvalidData,
932            format!(
933                "{label} {} does not register {initializer_name} as a preload constructor",
934                path.display()
935            ),
936        ));
937    }
938    path.canonicalize()
939}
940
941/// Environment variable naming the in-guest Detcore preload
942/// (`libdetcore_liteinst.so`) explicitly.
943pub const LITEINST_TOOL_RUNTIME_ENV: &str = "HERMIT_LITEINST_TOOL_RUNTIME";
944
945// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
946// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-3635): Review in-guest Detcore runtime discovery.
947/// Returns the in-guest Detcore preload: the one [`LITEINST_TOOL_RUNTIME_ENV`]
948/// names; otherwise the packaged `rsrcs/libdetcore_liteinst.so` of the selected
949/// Hermit installation (`hermit-install` stages it, as it stages the DBT and
950/// SaBRe Detcore runtimes, and a standalone installation or published bundle
951/// carries it); otherwise the one `cargo build -p detcore-liteinst` built beside
952/// the Hermit binary or in its `deps/` directory. Packaged resources come before
953/// the Cargo artifact directory, the order the DBT and SaBRe runtimes use.
954///
955/// A runtime built from another tree is refused when it starts: Hermit passes
956/// it [`detcore::config_wire_fingerprint`], which it compares with its own
957/// before decoding anything (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3986>).
958#[doc(hidden)]
959pub fn liteinst_tool_runtime_library_path() -> io::Result<PathBuf> {
960    if let Some(path) = std::env::var_os(LITEINST_TOOL_RUNTIME_ENV) {
961        let path = PathBuf::from(path);
962        if !path.is_file() {
963            return Err(io::Error::new(
964                io::ErrorKind::NotFound,
965                format!("{LITEINST_TOOL_RUNTIME_ENV} does not name a regular file"),
966            ));
967        }
968        return validate_liteinst_tool_runtime_library(&path).map_err(|error| {
969            io::Error::new(
970                error.kind(),
971                format!("{LITEINST_TOOL_RUNTIME_ENV} is invalid: {error}"),
972            )
973        });
974    }
975
976    if let Some(path) = hermit_resources::resource("libdetcore_liteinst.so")?
977        && path.is_file()
978    {
979        return validate_liteinst_tool_runtime_library(&path);
980    }
981
982    let executable = std::env::current_exe()?;
983    let directory = executable.parent().ok_or_else(|| {
984        io::Error::new(
985            io::ErrorKind::NotFound,
986            "Hermit executable has no parent directory",
987        )
988    })?;
989    if let Some(path) = [
990        directory.join("libdetcore_liteinst.so"),
991        directory.join("deps/libdetcore_liteinst.so"),
992    ]
993    .into_iter()
994    .find(|path| path.is_file())
995    {
996        return validate_liteinst_tool_runtime_library(&path);
997    }
998    Err(io::Error::new(
999        io::ErrorKind::NotFound,
1000        format!(
1001            "libdetcore_liteinst.so was not built beside {} or staged as an installed resource",
1002            executable.display()
1003        ),
1004    ))
1005}
1006
1007fn validate_liteinst_tool_runtime_library(path: &Path) -> io::Result<PathBuf> {
1008    validate_preload_constructor_library(
1009        path,
1010        "in-guest Detcore runtime",
1011        &["detcore_liteinst_initialize"],
1012        "detcore_liteinst_initialize",
1013    )
1014}

1016// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
1017// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-3635): Review the in-guest LiteInst library refusals.
1018/// In-guest LiteInst refused a run before starting its guest, because the
1019/// runtime cannot honour the request or could not monitor the program.
1020///
1021/// A type rather than a message, because it crosses the container error
1022/// boundary as [`error::FailureKind::PolicyRefusal`] (exit 122).
1023#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
1024pub struct LiteinstInGuestRefusal {
1025    backend: Backend,
1026    reason: String,
1027}
1028
1029impl LiteinstInGuestRefusal {
1030    /// A refusal of a `--backend=liteinst` run.
1031    pub fn new(reason: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
1032        Self::for_backend(Backend::Liteinst, reason)
1033    }
1034
1035    /// A refusal of a run of `backend`, one of the in-guest LiteInst backends
1036    /// ([`Backend::is_in_guest_liteinst`]), so the message names the backend
1037    /// the caller selected.
1038    pub fn for_backend(backend: Backend, reason: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
1039        debug_assert!(backend.is_in_guest_liteinst(), "{backend:?}");
1040        Self {
1041            backend,
1042            reason: reason.into(),
1043        }
1044    }
1045}
1046
1047impl std::fmt::Display for LiteinstInGuestRefusal {
1048    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
1049        let runtime = match self.backend {
1050            Backend::InGuestTrap => "in-guest LiteInst, site patching off",
1051            _ => "in-guest LiteInst",
1052        };
1053        write!(
1054            f,
1055            "--backend={} ({runtime}) refuses this run: {}",
1056            self.backend.as_str(),
1057            self.reason
1058        )
1059    }
1060}
1061
1062impl std::error::Error for LiteinstInGuestRefusal {}
1063
1064/// Refuses, before the guest starts, an in-guest LiteInst run that the runtime
1065/// cannot honour or could not monitor.
1066///
1067/// - A maximum timeslice. Reverie's in-guest Tool host has no preemption timer:
1068///   its `set_timer` refuses with `ENOSYS`, and Detcore treats a failed
1069///   `set_timer` as fatal (`.expect("Failed to set timer")`), so the guest
1070///   would panic at its first scheduling point. The CLI refuses
1071///   `--max-timeslice` earlier; this covers library callers.
1072/// - A kernel older than Linux 6.5 ([`in_guest_liteinst_kernel_gap`]).
1073/// - A guest program that would run code before, or without, the runtime's
1074///   constructor ([`in_guest_liteinst_program_gap`]).
1075/// - For `in-guest-trap`, a guest environment that sets
1076///   `REVERIE_LITEINST_SITE_PATCHING` to anything but `0`
1077///   ([`in_guest_trap_site_patching_conflict`]).
1078///
1079/// This runs where the guest is spawned (inside the container for the CLI),
1080/// so it resolves the program in the filesystem the guest sees. It pins the
1081/// resolved program on `command` ([`pin_spawn_program`]), so the launch
1082/// executes the same path this check inspected. For `in-guest-trap` it then
1083/// sets `REVERIE_LITEINST_SITE_PATCHING=0` on `command`
1084/// ([`force_in_guest_site_patching`]).
1085#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1086fn refuse_in_guest_liteinst_run(
1087    command: &mut Command,
1088    config: &DetConfig,
1089    backend: Backend,
1090) -> Result<(), Error> {
1091    let reason = if let Some(conflict) = in_guest_trap_site_patching_conflict(command, backend) {
1092        Some(conflict)
1093    } else if config.max_timeslice.is_some() {
1094        Some(
1095            "it cannot deliver Detcore's preemption timer yet (the in-guest Tool host refuses \
1096             set_timer with ENOSYS), so the run would fail at its first timeslice; pass \
1097             --max-timeslice=disabled"
1098                .to_owned(),
1099        )
1100    } else if let Some(gap) = in_guest_liteinst_kernel_gap(&running_kernel_release()) {
1101        Some(gap)
1102    } else {
1103        in_guest_liteinst_program_gap(command)?
1104    };
1105    match reason {
1106        Some(reason) => Err(Error::new(LiteinstInGuestRefusal::for_backend(
1107            backend, reason,
1108        ))),
1109        None => {
1110            force_in_guest_site_patching(command, backend);
1111            Ok(())
1112        }
1113    }
1114}
1115
1116/// The value of `REVERIE_LITEINST_SITE_PATCHING` (`reverie_liteinst::SITE_PATCHING_ENV`)
1117/// that the guest sees under `backend`, when Hermit decides it: `0` for
1118/// `in-guest-trap`, which exists to run with no syscall site patched, and
1119/// `None` for `liteinst`, which leaves the variable to the guest environment
1120/// (unset means patching on).
1121pub fn forced_site_patching_value(backend: Backend) -> Option<&'static str> {
1122    match backend {
1123        Backend::InGuestTrap => Some("0"),
1124        _ => None,
1125    }
1126}
1127
1128/// Why an `in-guest-trap` run cannot start with the guest environment of
1129/// `command`: the environment (explicit or inherited) sets
1130/// `REVERIE_LITEINST_SITE_PATCHING` to a value other than the one Hermit
1131/// forces. Hermit refuses such a run rather than overriding the value
1132/// silently, because the caller asked for two contradictory things: site
1133/// patching (or a value the runtime would reject) and the backend that exists
1134/// to run without it. A value equal to the forced one is accepted. `None`
1135/// for every other backend.
1136#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1137fn in_guest_trap_site_patching_conflict(command: &Command, backend: Backend) -> Option<String> {
1138    let forced = forced_site_patching_value(backend)?;
1139    let name = reverie_liteinst::SITE_PATCHING_ENV;
1140    let value = command.get_env(name)?;
1141    (*value != *std::ffi::OsStr::new(forced)).then(|| {
1142        format!(
1143            "the guest environment sets {name}={}, but --backend={} runs with syscall site \
1144             patching off and sets {name}={forced} itself; drop the variable, or select \
1145             --backend=liteinst to patch syscall sites",
1146            value.to_string_lossy(),
1147            backend.as_str()
1148        )
1149    })
1150}
1151
1152/// Sets the guest's `REVERIE_LITEINST_SITE_PATCHING` to the value Hermit
1153/// forces for `backend` ([`forced_site_patching_value`]), leaving the
1154/// environment unchanged for a backend that forces none. The guest's
1155/// descendants inherit it unless the guest changes its own environment; a
1156/// descendant that turns patching back on shows in the run's dispatch record
1157/// as patched sites.
1158#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1159fn force_in_guest_site_patching(command: &mut Command, backend: Backend) {
1160    if let Some(value) = forced_site_patching_value(backend) {
1161        command.env(reverie_liteinst::SITE_PATCHING_ENV, value);
1162    }
1163}
1164
1165/// Whether every in-guest Tool image of a `backend` run checks that its runtime
1166/// captured site patching off and statistics on
1167/// (`detcore::Config::in_guest_site_patching_off`): true for `in-guest-trap`.
1168#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1169fn in_guest_site_patching_off(backend: Backend) -> bool {
1170    forced_site_patching_value(backend) == Some("0")
1171}
1172
1173/// Whether an in-guest run of `backend` collects LiteInst's statistics: when
1174/// something reads them ([`backend_stats::request`]), and on every
1175/// `in-guest-trap` run, whose result is checked against them
1176/// ([`in_guest_trap_patching_evidence`]).
1177#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1178fn in_guest_stats_request(
1179    backend: Backend,
1180    summary_json: &Option<PathBuf>,
1181) -> reverie::BackendStatsRequest {
1182    if backend == Backend::InGuestTrap {
1183        reverie::BackendStatsRequest::ENABLED
1184    } else {
1185        backend_stats::request(summary_json)
1186    }
1187}
1188
1189/// Why a finished `in-guest-trap` run did not show that it ran with syscall
1190/// site patching off, from its dispatch record and the number of guest
1191/// processes that reported LiteInst statistics: a site the runtime patched or
1192/// considered patching (with patching off it considers none), no record, or no
1193/// process report at all, in which case the record's zeros are not
1194/// measurements (a run whose guests collected no statistics still yields an
1195/// empty record). `None` for every other backend.
1196///
1197/// This is the coordinator's postcondition. Each Tool image also refuses to
1198/// run its program when the runtime started it with patching on
1199/// (`detcore::in_guest_site_patching`), which holds for every image whatever
1200/// it reports; this one sees only the images that report, because an image
1201/// reports its statistics when it exits (`exit` or `exit_group`), so an image
1202/// that `execve`s, or is killed by a signal, sends none, and the coordinator
1203/// cannot tell that from a report that was suppressed. It refuses a run in
1204/// which no process reported; it does not detect a run in which some did and
1205/// others did not. Each image's own check covers that: an image whose runtime
1206/// is not collecting statistics refuses to run its program.
1207#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1208fn in_guest_trap_patching_evidence(
1209    backend: Backend,
1210    dispatch: Option<&reverie::DispatchStats>,
1211    process_reports: u64,
1212) -> Option<String> {
1213    if backend != Backend::InGuestTrap {
1214        return None;
1215    }
1216    let Some(record) = dispatch else {
1217        return Some(
1218            "the run produced no LiteInst dispatch record, so it cannot show that no syscall \
1219             site was patched"
1220                .to_owned(),
1221        );
1222    };
1223    if process_reports == 0 {
1224        return Some(
1225            "no guest process reported LiteInst statistics, so the run's zero patched sites \
1226             are not a measurement and it cannot show that no syscall site was patched"
1227                .to_owned(),
1228        );
1229    }
1230    let shown =
1231        |count: Option<u64>| count.map_or_else(|| "unmeasured".to_owned(), |n| n.to_string());
1232    (record.sites.patched != Some(0) || record.sites.candidates != Some(0)).then(|| {
1233        format!(
1234            "its guests' LiteInst statistics report {} patched and {} candidate syscall sites, \
1235             so it did not run with syscall site patching off and its result is not an \
1236             in-guest-trap result",
1237            shown(record.sites.patched),
1238            shown(record.sites.candidates)
1239        )
1240    })
1241}
1242
1243/// Refuses a finished `in-guest-trap` run whose dispatch record shows site
1244/// patching ([`in_guest_trap_patching_evidence`]): records a determinism loss,
1245/// so verification refuses to compare it, and returns the typed in-guest
1246/// refusal (exit 122).
1247#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1248fn refuse_in_guest_trap_patching(backend: Backend, evidence: Option<String>) -> Result<(), Error> {
1249    match evidence {
1250        Some(reason) => {
1251            detcore::detlog::record_determinism_loss(&reason);
1252            Err(Error::new(LiteinstInGuestRefusal::for_backend(
1253                backend, reason,
1254            )))
1255        }
1256        None => Ok(()),
1257    }
1258}
1259
1260/// The oldest kernel in-guest LiteInst runs on, as (major, minor). Linux 6.5
1261/// added `SO_PEERPIDFD`: the coordinator admits each guest process through the
1262/// pidfd of the socket's peer and watches that pidfd for the process's exit
1263/// (`in_guest_exits`). On an older kernel every admission would fail.
1264#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1265const IN_GUEST_LITEINST_KERNEL_FLOOR: (u32, u32) = (6, 5);
1266
1267/// The running kernel's release string (`uname -r`), or empty if unreadable.
1268#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1269fn running_kernel_release() -> String {
1270    let mut name: libc::utsname = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed() };
1271    // SAFETY: uname fills the struct it is given.
1272    if unsafe { libc::uname(&mut name) } != 0 {
1273        return String::new();
1274    }
1275    // SAFETY: uname NUL-terminates each field.
1276    unsafe { std::ffi::CStr::from_ptr(name.release.as_ptr()) }
1277        .to_string_lossy()
1278        .into_owned()
1279}
1280
1281/// Why kernel `release` cannot run in-guest LiteInst, or `None` when it is at
1282/// least [`IN_GUEST_LITEINST_KERNEL_FLOOR`]. A release whose version cannot be
1283/// read is refused.
1284#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1285fn in_guest_liteinst_kernel_gap(release: &str) -> Option<String> {
1286    let mut numbers = release
1287        .split(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_digit())
1288        .map(|part| part.parse::<u32>().ok());
1289    let (floor_major, floor_minor) = IN_GUEST_LITEINST_KERNEL_FLOOR;
1290    match (numbers.next().flatten(), numbers.next().flatten()) {
1291        (Some(major), Some(minor)) if (major, minor) >= IN_GUEST_LITEINST_KERNEL_FLOOR => None,
1292        (Some(_), Some(_)) => Some(format!(
1293            "it needs Linux {floor_major}.{floor_minor} or later (SO_PEERPIDFD, which it uses to \
1294             admit each guest process and observe its exit); this kernel is {release}"
1295        )),
1296        _ => Some(format!(
1297            "it needs Linux {floor_major}.{floor_minor} or later (SO_PEERPIDFD), and the kernel \
1298             release {release:?} does not say which version this is"
1299        )),
1300    }
1301}
1302
1303/// How many `#!` interpreters [`in_guest_liteinst_program_gap`] follows before
1304/// it refuses the program. The kernel has its own small limit (`exec_binprm` in
1305/// `fs/exec.c`); a chain this check stops following is refused, not run, so the
1306/// check fails closed whichever limit is lower.
1307#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1308const MAX_INTERPRETER_DEPTH: usize = 4;
1309
1310/// Why the guest program would run code before, or without, the in-guest
1311/// runtime, or `None` when it would not.
1312///
1313/// The runtime is an `LD_PRELOAD` library whose constructor installs Detcore.
1314/// The dynamic loader never loads it into a statically linked image or an image
1315/// of another architecture, and ignores `LD_PRELOAD` in secure-execution mode
1316/// (set-user-ID, set-group-ID, or file capabilities). An executable's
1317/// `DT_PREINIT_ARRAY` runs before every library constructor. Each of those
1318/// would run guest code unmonitored, so each is refused. `#!` scripts are
1319/// checked through their interpreter, read as the kernel's script handler
1320/// reads it ([`script::KernelScript`]). A program or interpreter that is not a
1321/// regular file is refused before it is opened: the kernel executes only
1322/// regular files, opening a device can act on it, opening a socket fails, and
1323/// reading a FIFO that has no writer would block the check.
1324///
1325/// Every fact about one file (its type, header, ELF headers, mode bits and
1326/// capabilities) is read through one open descriptor, so they all describe
1327/// the same file. The check is still advisory, not containment: the `execve`
1328/// opens the path again, and a file replaced at that path in between is not
1329/// checked again.
1330///
1331/// Not covered, and recorded on
1332/// <https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3520>:
1333/// - The loader itself, the C library's initialization, the executable's
1334///   `IFUNC` resolvers and the constructors of the executable's own shared
1335///   libraries all run before the runtime's constructor in every guest.
1336/// - Secure-execution mode that a security module or a parent with differing
1337///   real and effective IDs imposes; only the file's own mode bits and
1338///   capabilities are checked.
1339/// - Whether the `PT_INTERP` loader honours `LD_PRELOAD` at all.
1340/// - A `binfmt_misc` rule, which the kernel consults before its ELF and script
1341///   handlers, and which can hand a file this check accepted to another
1342///   interpreter (for example an emulator).
1343/// - A runtime library the loader fails to load: the loader continues without
1344///   it, and only the launch's check that the guest connected before it exited
1345///   reports the failure, after the guest ran.
1346/// - A `pre_exec` callback on `command` that changes the working directory or
1347///   the root directory: the check resolves paths against the working
1348///   directory `command` is configured with, in Hermit's root, before any
1349///   such callback runs.
1350#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1351fn in_guest_liteinst_program_gap(command: &mut Command) -> Result<Option<String>, Error> {
1352    use std::io::Read;
1353    use std::os::unix::fs::OpenOptionsExt;
1354
1355    use script::KernelScript;
1356
1357    let mut path = pin_spawn_program(command)?;
1358    for _ in 0..=MAX_INTERPRETER_DEPTH {
1359        let shown = path.display().to_string();
1360        let not_regular = || {
1361            Ok(Some(format!(
1362                "{shown} is not a regular file, and the kernel executes only regular files"
1363            )))
1364        };
1365        if !fs::metadata(&path)
1366            .with_context(|| format!("cannot read guest program {shown}"))?
1367            .is_file()
1368        {
1369            return not_regular();
1370        }
1371        // The path can change type after the check above. O_NONBLOCK then keeps
1372        // the open of a FIFO that has no writer from blocking, and O_NOCTTY
1373        // keeps a terminal from becoming Hermit's controlling terminal; neither
1374        // changes how a regular file is read. The descriptor's own type is
1375        // checked again below.
1376        let mut file = fs::OpenOptions::new()
1377            .read(true)
1378            .custom_flags(libc::O_NONBLOCK | libc::O_NOCTTY)
1379            .open(&path)
1380            .with_context(|| format!("cannot read guest program {shown}"))?;
1381        if !file
1382            .metadata()
1383            .with_context(|| format!("cannot read guest program {shown}"))?
1384            .is_file()
1385        {
1386            return not_regular();
1387        }
1388        let mut header = Vec::new();
1389        (&file)
1390            .take(256)
1391            .read_to_end(&mut header)
1392            .with_context(|| format!("cannot read guest program {shown}"))?;
1393        match KernelScript::from_buf(&header) {
1394            KernelScript::Interpreter(interpreter) => {
1395                // The kernel opens a relative interpreter against the working
1396                // directory of the process calling execve.
1397                path = match command.get_current_dir() {
1398                    Some(directory) => directory.join(interpreter),
1399                    None => interpreter,
1400                };
1401                continue;
1402            }
1403            KernelScript::Declined => {
1404                return Ok(Some(format!(
1405                    "{shown} starts with #! but the kernel's script handler declines it (it \
1406                     names no interpreter, or the interpreter path fills the first 256 bytes), \
1407                     so the kernel would fail the execve or hand the file to another handler \
1408                     that need not load the runtime"
1409                )));
1410            }
1411            KernelScript::NotScript => {}
1412        }
1413        let Some(startup) = interp::elf_startup(&mut file)
1414            .with_context(|| format!("cannot read the ELF headers of guest program {shown}"))?
1415        else {
1416            return Ok(Some(format!(
1417                "{shown} is neither an ELF executable nor a #! script, so the kernel would hand \
1418                 it to a handler that need not load the runtime"
1419            )));
1420        };
1421        let reason = if !startup.x86_64 {
1422            "is not a 64-bit x86-64 ELF image, so the dynamic loader cannot load the 64-bit \
1423             runtime into it"
1424        } else if !startup.has_interp {
1425            "is statically linked (it has no PT_INTERP), so no dynamic loader runs to load the \
1426             runtime"
1427        } else if is_secure_execution_image(&file, &shown)? {
1428            "is set-user-ID, set-group-ID or has file capabilities, so the kernel runs it in \
1429             secure-execution mode, where the dynamic loader ignores LD_PRELOAD"
1430        } else if startup.has_preinit_array {
1431            "has a DT_PREINIT_ARRAY, whose functions run before the runtime's constructor"
1432        } else {
1433            return Ok(None);
1434        };
1435        return Ok(Some(format!(
1436            "guest program {shown} {reason}, so it would run unmonitored if Hermit launched it"
1437        )));
1438    }
1439    Ok(Some(format!(
1440        "the #! interpreters of guest program {} nest more than {MAX_INTERPRETER_DEPTH} deep",
1441        Path::new(command.get_program()).display()
1442    )))
1443}
1444
1445/// Resolves the program `command` executes with Reverie's
1446/// [`Command::find_program`], the call the in-guest launch makes, and pins the
1447/// result on `command`, keeping `argv[0]`.
1448///
1449/// The result is absolute, so the launch's own `find_program` returns it
1450/// unchanged and the launch executes the same path the check inspected,
1451/// whatever `PATH` or working directory either side would otherwise use. The
1452/// path, not its contents: a file replaced at that path in between is not
1453/// checked again (see [`in_guest_liteinst_program_gap`]). A program the launch
1454/// could not find is an error here, as it would be at launch.
1455#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1456fn pin_spawn_program(command: &mut Command) -> Result<PathBuf, Error> {
1457    let arg0 = command.get_arg0().to_owned();
1458    let program = command.find_program().with_context(|| {
1459        format!(
1460            "cannot find guest program {}",
1461            Path::new(command.get_program()).display()
1462        )
1463    })?;
1464    command.program(&program).arg0(arg0);
1465    Ok(program)
1466}
1467
1468/// Whether executing `file` (shown as `shown`) puts the kernel in
1469/// secure-execution mode (`AT_SECURE`): set-user-ID, set-group-ID (with group
1470/// execute, as the kernel requires), or a `security.capability` attribute.
1471#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1472fn is_secure_execution_image(file: &fs::File, shown: &str) -> Result<bool, Error> {
1473    let mode = file
1474        .metadata()
1475        .with_context(|| format!("cannot stat guest program {shown}"))?
1476        .permissions()
1477        .mode();
1478    if mode & 0o4000 != 0 || mode & 0o2010 == 0o2010 {
1479        return Ok(true);
1480    }
1481    // SAFETY: the descriptor is open for the call, the name is NUL-terminated,
1482    // and a zero-sized query writes no value.
1483    let size = unsafe {
1484        libc::fgetxattr(
1485            file.as_raw_fd(),
1486            c"security.capability".as_ptr(),
1487            std::ptr::null_mut(),
1488            0,
1489        )
1490    };
1491    if size >= 0 {
1492        return Ok(true);
1493    }
1494    match io::Error::last_os_error().raw_os_error() {
1495        Some(libc::ENODATA) | Some(libc::ENOTSUP) => Ok(false),
1496        _ => Err(Error::new(io::Error::last_os_error()).context(format!(
1497            "cannot read the file capabilities of guest program {shown}"
1498        ))),
1499    }
1500}
1501
1502/// Refuses a wall-clock bound on an in-guest LiteInst run. The bound drops the
1503/// run's future, and dropping it neither kills the guest nor stops the
1504/// blocking task that waits for it.
1505#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1506fn refuse_in_guest_liteinst_timeout(
1507    backend: Backend,
1508    timeout: Option<Duration>,
1509) -> Result<(), Error> {
1510    if backend.is_in_guest_liteinst() && timeout.is_some() {
1511        return Err(Error::new(LiteinstInGuestRefusal::for_backend(
1512            backend,
1513            "a wall-clock timeout has not been qualified for the in-guest runtime, whose run \
1514             future does not kill the guest when it is dropped",
1515        )));
1516    }
1517    Ok(())
1518}
1519
1520#[cfg(not(feature = "liteinst"))]
1521fn refuse_in_guest_liteinst_timeout(
1522    _backend: Backend,
1523    _timeout: Option<Duration>,
1524) -> Result<(), Error> {
1525    Ok(())
1526}
1527
1528#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1529fn liteinst_unavailable_reason() -> Option<String> {
1530    liteinst_runtime_unavailable_reason()
1531}
1532
1533// LiteInst is an experimental backend whose architecture is not settled, so the
1534// published crate leaves it out: `reverie-liteinst` (and through it `liteinst2`)
1535// is an optional dependency behind the `liteinst` feature, which
1536// `third-party-backends` enables for every validation build.
1537#[cfg(not(feature = "liteinst"))]
1538fn liteinst_unavailable_reason() -> Option<String> {
1539    Some("this build was compiled without the liteinst backend: the `liteinst` feature is not enabled; rebuild with `--features liteinst` (or `--features third-party-backends`). This says nothing about whether LiteInst works on this machine -- it has not been checked".to_owned())
1540}
1541
1542#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1543fn liteinst_runtime_unavailable_reason() -> Option<String> {
1544    liteinst_tool_runtime_library_path().err().map(|error| {
1545        format!(
1546            "the in-guest Detcore runtime is unavailable: {error}; build it with `cargo build -p detcore-liteinst` in the target directory of this hermit binary, install it as rsrcs/libdetcore_liteinst.so, or name it with {LITEINST_TOOL_RUNTIME_ENV}"
1547        )
1548    })
1549}
1550
1551fn kvm_device_unavailable_reason(path: &Path) -> Option<String> {
1552    fs::OpenOptions::new()
1553        .read(true)
1554        .write(true)
1555        .open(path)
1556        .err()
1557        .map(|error| {
1558            format!(
1559                "cannot open {} read-write: {error}; grant access through the device owner/group \
1560                 or root",
1561                path.display()
1562            )
1563        })
1564}
1565
1566/// Process instrumentation backend used to run a Hermit guest.
1567#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, Eq, PartialEq, ValueEnum)]
1568pub enum Backend {
1569    /// Use Reverie's ptrace backend.
1570    #[default]
1571    Ptrace,
1572    /// Use the DynamoRIO backend.
1573    Dbt,
1574    /// Use LiteInst, with Detcore's Tool running inside the guest.
1575    Liteinst,
1576    /// Use in-guest LiteInst with syscall site patching off: the same
1577    /// in-guest Detcore host as `liteinst`, but every trapped syscall reaches
1578    /// the Tool through the seccomp `SIGSYS` fallback and no syscall site is
1579    /// rewritten. Hermit sets `REVERIE_LITEINST_SITE_PATCHING=0` in the
1580    /// guest's environment and refuses a run whose environment asks for any
1581    /// other value.
1582    InGuestTrap,
1583    /// Use the SaBRe static binary rewriting backend.
1584    Sabre,
1585    /// Use the KVM backend.
1586    Kvm,
1587    /// Preprocess the main ELF with e9patch, then use the ptrace runtime.
1588    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-594): Review the CLI-only hybrid backend selection.
1589    E9patch,
1590}
1591
1592/// A requested backend could not start because its required runtime support is absent.
1593///
1594/// Keep this as a typed error until the top-level command records its failure class.
1595/// The human-readable message is for the operator; consumers must use the class line
1596/// emitted by the command rather than matching this prose.
1597#[derive(Debug, Clone, Eq, PartialEq)]
1598pub struct BackendUnavailable {
1599    backend: Backend,
1600    reason: String,
1601}
1602
1603impl BackendUnavailable {
1604    pub fn new(backend: Backend, reason: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
1605        Self {
1606            backend,
1607            reason: reason.into(),
1608        }
1609    }
1610
1611    pub fn backend(&self) -> Backend {
1612        self.backend
1613    }
1614
1615    pub fn reason(&self) -> &str {
1616        &self.reason
1617    }
1618}
1619
1620impl std::fmt::Display for BackendUnavailable {
1621    fn fmt(&self, formatter: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
1622        write!(
1623            formatter,
1624            "backend `{}` is unavailable: {}",
1625            self.backend.as_str(),
1626            self.reason
1627        )
1628    }
1629}
1630
1631impl std::error::Error for BackendUnavailable {}
1632
1633impl Backend {
1634    const ALL: [Self; 7] = [
1635        Self::Ptrace,
1636        Self::Dbt,
1637        Self::Liteinst,
1638        Self::InGuestTrap,
1639        Self::Sabre,
1640        Self::Kvm,
1641        Self::E9patch,
1642    ];
1643
1644    /// Returns the command-line spelling for this backend.
1645    pub const fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
1646        match self {
1647            Self::Ptrace => "ptrace",
1648            Self::Dbt => "dbt",
1649            Self::Liteinst => "liteinst",
1650            Self::InGuestTrap => "in-guest-trap",
1651            Self::Sabre => "sabre",
1652            Self::Kvm => "kvm",
1653            Self::E9patch => "e9patch",
1654        }
1655    }
1656
1657    /// Whether this backend hosts Detcore's Tool inside each guest process
1658    /// through the in-guest LiteInst preload runtime: `liteinst` and
1659    /// `in-guest-trap`, which differ only in whether syscall sites are patched.
1660    /// Every refusal, capability and launch step of in-guest LiteInst applies to
1661    /// both.
1662    pub const fn is_in_guest_liteinst(self) -> bool {
1663        matches!(self, Self::Liteinst | Self::InGuestTrap)
1664    }
1665
1666    /// The name of the backend runtime that executes this backend's guest, as
1667    /// its statistics source names itself (`BackendStatsSnapshot::BACKEND_NAME`).
1668    /// `in-guest-trap` runs the in-guest LiteInst runtime, so its statistics come
1669    /// from that runtime's source, which is named `liteinst`.
1670    pub const fn runtime_name(self) -> &'static str {
1671        match self {
1672            Self::InGuestTrap => Self::Liteinst.as_str(),
1673            other => other.as_str(),
1674        }
1675    }
1676
1677    fn uses_ptrace_pmu_timers(self) -> bool {
1678        match self {
1679            Self::Ptrace | Self::E9patch => true,
1680            // In-guest Detcore has no ptrace tracer to arm the PMU timer from.
1681            Self::Liteinst | Self::InGuestTrap | Self::Dbt | Self::Sabre | Self::Kvm => false,
1682        }
1683    }
1684
1685    /// Returns backends whose Hermit integration prerequisites are met.
1686    ///
1687    /// Some integrations use CLI launch adapters rather than direct
1688    /// [`run_with_backend`] dispatch.
1689    pub fn available() -> impl Iterator<Item = Self> {
1690        Self::ALL
1691            .into_iter()
1692            .filter(|backend| backend.is_available())
1693    }
1694
1695    /// Returns whether this backend's integration prerequisites are met.
1696    pub fn is_available(self) -> bool {
1697        self.unavailable_reason().is_none()
1698    }
1699
1700    /// Returns an actionable error when this backend's prerequisites are not met.
1701    pub fn ensure_available(self) -> Result<(), Error> {
1702        if let Some(reason) = self.unavailable_reason() {
1703            Err(Error::new(BackendUnavailable::new(self, reason)))
1704        } else {
1705            Ok(())
1706        }
1707    }
1708
1709    fn unavailable_reason(self) -> Option<String> {
1710        match self {
1711            Self::Ptrace => validate_tracing_environment()
1712                .err()
1713                .map(|error| error.to_string()),
1714            Self::Dbt => dbt_unavailable_reason(),
1715            // The same runtime, so the same prerequisites and the same reason.
1716            Self::Liteinst | Self::InGuestTrap => liteinst_unavailable_reason(),
1717            // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(#589): Review SaBRe backend availability reporting.
1718            Self::Sabre => sabre_unavailable_reason(),
1719            Self::Kvm => kvm_device_unavailable_reason(Path::new("/dev/kvm")),
1720            Self::E9patch => e9patch_unavailable_reason(),
1721        }
1722    }
1723}
1724
1725struct SkidOvershootReport {
1726    enabled: bool,
1727}
1728
1729/// A ptrace-backed run observed late precise-timer delivery, so Hermit refuses
1730/// to treat the completed execution as deterministic evidence.
1731///
1732/// This is a type rather than a message match because it crosses the container
1733/// error boundary as [`error::FailureKind::PolicyRefusal`].
1734#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
1735pub struct SkidOvershootError {
1736    count: u64,
1737}
1738
1739impl SkidOvershootError {
1740    pub fn new(count: u64) -> Self {
1741        assert!(
1742            count > 0,
1743            "a skid-overshoot refusal requires a positive count"
1744        );
1745        Self { count }
1746    }
1747
1748    pub fn count(&self) -> u64 {
1749        self.count
1750    }
1751}
1752
1753impl std::fmt::Display for SkidOvershootError {
1754    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
1755        write!(
1756            f,
1757            "observed {} {} report(s); refusing the result because precise PMU timer \
1758             delivery passed its target and deterministic execution was not established",
1759            self.count,
1760            reverie::SKID_OVERSHOOT_MARKER
1761        )
1762    }
1763}
1764
1765impl std::error::Error for SkidOvershootError {}
1766
1767fn take_skid_overshoot_error() -> Option<SkidOvershootError> {
1768    let count = reverie::take_skid_overshoot_count();
1769    (count > 0).then(|| SkidOvershootError::new(count))
1770}
1771
1772impl SkidOvershootReport {
1773    fn begin(enabled: bool) -> Self {
1774        if enabled {
1775            // A previous invocation in this process must not qualify this one.
1776            let _ = reverie::take_skid_overshoot_count();
1777        }
1778        Self { enabled }
1779    }
1780
1781    fn finish<T>(self, result: Result<T, Error>) -> Result<T, Error> {
1782        if !self.enabled {
1783            return result;
1784        }
1785
1786        let Some(overshoot) = take_skid_overshoot_error() else {
1787            return result;
1788        };
1789        match result {
1790            Ok(_) => Err(Error::new(overshoot)),
1791            Err(error) => Err(error.context(overshoot)),
1792        }
1793    }
1794
1795    fn finish_with_count<T>(self, result: Result<T, Error>) -> Result<(T, u64), Error> {
1796        if !self.enabled {
1797            return result.map(|value| (value, 0));
1798        }
1799
1800        let count = reverie::take_skid_overshoot_count();
1801        match result {
1802            Ok(value) => Ok((value, count)),
1803            Err(error) if count > 0 => Err(error.context(SkidOvershootError::new(count))),
1804            Err(error) => Err(error),
1805        }
1806    }
1807}

1810// loader and Detcore plugin must also be supplied. e9patch preprocessing requires

1820}

1825        .err()

1827        .or_else(e9patch::unavailable_reason)

1832    Some("the `e9patch` feature is not enabled in this build; rebuild with `--features e9patch` (or `--features third-party-backends`). This says nothing about whether e9patch works on this machine -- it has not been checked".to_owned())
1833}

1858    } else {
1859        "the `dbt` feature is not enabled in this source build; rebuild with `--features dbt` (or `--features third-party-backends`). DynamoRIO availability on this machine has not been checked".to_owned()
1860    }
