1863#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
1864const SABRE_BINARY_ENV: &str = "HERMIT_SABRE_BINARY";
1865
1866#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
1867fn is_executable_file(path: &Path) -> bool {
1868    fs::metadata(path)
1869        .is_ok_and(|metadata| metadata.is_file() && metadata.permissions().mode() & 0o111 != 0)
1870}
1871
1872// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-739): Review SaBRe loader discovery and executable validation.
1873#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
1874fn resolve_sabre_binary_from(
1875    override_path: Option<&OsStr>,
1876    packaged_path: Option<&Path>,
1877    executable: &Path,
1878    path_env: &OsStr,
1879) -> Result<PathBuf, Error> {
1880    if let Some(requested) = override_path {
1881        if requested.is_empty() {
1882            return Err(anyhow!("{SABRE_BINARY_ENV} is empty"));
1883        }
1884        let path = PathBuf::from(requested);
1885        return is_executable_file(&path)
1886            .then_some(path.clone())
1887            .ok_or_else(|| {
1888                anyhow!(
1889                    "{SABRE_BINARY_ENV}={} is not an executable file",
1890                    path.display()
1891                )
1892            });
1893    }
1894
1895    if let Some(path) = packaged_path
1896        && is_executable_file(path)
1897    {
1898        return Ok(path.to_path_buf());
1899    }
1900
1901    let directory = executable
1902        .parent()
1903        .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("Hermit executable has no parent directory"))?;
1904    let sibling = directory.join("sabre");
1905    let target_build = directory.parent().map(|target| target.join("sabre/sabre"));
1906
1907    if is_executable_file(&sibling) {
1908        return Ok(sibling);
1909    }
1910    if let Some(candidate) = &target_build
1911        && is_executable_file(candidate)
1912    {
1913        return Ok(candidate.clone());
1914    }
1915    if !path_env.is_empty()
1916        && let Some(candidate) = std::env::split_paths(path_env)
1917            .map(|directory| directory.join("sabre"))
1918            .find(|candidate| is_executable_file(candidate))
1919    {
1920        return Ok(candidate);
1921    }
1922
1923    Err(anyhow!(
1924        "SaBRe executable was not found in the Hermit installation, beside {}, or in PATH; set {} or {SABRE_BINARY_ENV}",
1925        executable.display(),
1926        hermit_resources::INSTALL_DIR_ENV
1927    ))
1928}
1929
1930#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
1931fn resolve_sabre_binary() -> Result<PathBuf, Error> {
1932    let executable =
1933        std::env::current_exe().context("failed to locate running Hermit executable")?;
1934    let override_path = std::env::var_os(SABRE_BINARY_ENV);
1935    let packaged_path = hermit_resources::resource("sabre")?;
1936    let path_env = std::env::var_os("PATH").unwrap_or_default();
1937    resolve_sabre_binary_from(
1938        override_path.as_deref(),
1939        packaged_path.as_deref(),
1940        &executable,
1941        &path_env,
1942    )
1943}

1945#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
1946const SABRE_RPC_SOCKET_ENV: &str = "REVERIE_SABRE_HERMIT_RPC_SOCKET";
1947#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
1948const SABRE_DETLOG_FORWARD_ENV: &str = "REVERIE_SABRE_HERMIT_FORWARD_DETLOG";
1949/// `reverie_sabre::TOOL_OUTPUT_ENV`: the descriptor reverie-sabre protects
1950/// for the in-guest tool's own output. Hermit does not link reverie-sabre.
1951#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
1952const SABRE_TOOL_OUTPUT_ENV: &str = "REVERIE_SABRE_TOOL_OUTPUT_FD";
1953/// `detcore_liteinst::DETLOG_FORWARD_ENV`; that crate is a preload `cdylib`, so
1954/// Hermit cannot import the constant.
1955#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1956const LITEINST_DETLOG_FORWARD_ENV: &str = "HERMIT_LITEINST_FORWARD_DETLOG";
1957/// `detcore_liteinst::DETLOG_FORWARD_POLICY_ENV`, for the same reason.
1958#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1959const LITEINST_DETLOG_FORWARD_POLICY_ENV: &str = "HERMIT_LITEINST_FORWARD_DETLOG_POLICY";
1960
1961/// Tells the in-guest LiteInst runtime this build's
1962/// [`detcore::config_wire_fingerprint`], which it compares with its own before
1963/// it decodes the configuration the coordinator sends. A runtime built from
1964/// another tree then fails naming both fingerprints and its own path, not with
1965/// a bare decoding error (<https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3986>).
1966#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1967fn announce_liteinst_config_fingerprint(command: &mut Command) {
1968    command.env(
1969        detcore::LITEINST_CONFIG_FINGERPRINT_ENV,
1970        detcore::config_wire_fingerprint(),
1971    );
1972}
1973
1974/// Where in-guest LiteInst and SaBRe runs in this process forward Detcore's
1975/// DETLOG records; see [`forward_in_guest_detlogs_to`].
1976static IN_GUEST_DETLOG_SINK: Mutex<Option<std::os::fd::OwnedFd>> = Mutex::new(None);
1977
1978/// Makes the in-guest LiteInst and SaBRe runs this process starts forward the
1979/// DETLOG records Detcore logs inside the guest to `socket`, the sending end of a Unix
1980/// `SOCK_SEQPACKET` pair. `hermit run --verify` gives each run its own pair and
1981/// reads the records from the other end into that run's log. Each record is one
1982/// message, one line (`detcore::detlog::forwarded_line`).
1983/// A record is forwarded when the CLI's filter logs INFO for its module (see
1984/// [`in_guest_detlog_forward_policy`]); without a socket none are forwarded.
1985#[doc(hidden)]
1986pub fn forward_in_guest_detlogs_to(socket: std::os::fd::OwnedFd) {
1987    *IN_GUEST_DETLOG_SINK.lock().unwrap() = Some(socket);
1988}
1989
1990/// Hands the in-guest LiteInst runtime the socket chosen with
1991/// [`forward_in_guest_detlogs_to`], when `policy` forwards any record: an
1992/// inheritable duplicate whose number goes in the private opt-in variable, and
1993/// the encoded policy, which the guest's Detcore applies to each record by its
1994/// module as each in-process `detlog!` callsite asks tracing. The runtime
1995/// moves the socket to a descriptor Reverie reserves and protects from the
1996/// guest. The caller keeps the returned duplicate open until the guest has
1997/// started.
1998#[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
1999fn request_liteinst_detlog_forwarding(
2000    command: &mut Command,
2001    policy: &detcore::detlog::ForwardPolicy,
2002) -> Result<Option<std::os::fd::OwnedFd>, Error> {
2003    command.env_remove(LITEINST_DETLOG_FORWARD_ENV);
2004    command.env_remove(LITEINST_DETLOG_FORWARD_POLICY_ENV);
2005    // Asking only whether INFO is on for `detcore`, and then forwarding
2006    // everything, kept records a target-scoped filter drops under ptrace
2007    // (info,detcore::random=warn) and could forward nothing where it keeps
2008    // some (warn,detcore::random=info).
2009    if !policy.forwards_any() {
2010        return Ok(None);
2011    }
2012    let Some(duplicate) = inheritable_in_guest_detlog_socket()? else {
2013        return Ok(None);
2014    };
2015    command.env(
2016        LITEINST_DETLOG_FORWARD_ENV,
2017        detcore::detlog::tool_output_env_value(duplicate.as_raw_fd())?,
2018    );
2019    command.env(LITEINST_DETLOG_FORWARD_POLICY_ENV, policy.encode());
2020    Ok(Some(duplicate))
2021}
2022
2023/// An inheritable duplicate of the socket chosen with
2024/// [`forward_in_guest_detlogs_to`], or `None` when none was chosen. The
2025/// caller passes its number to the guest and keeps it open until the guest
2026/// has started.
2027#[cfg(any(feature = "sabre", feature = "liteinst"))]
2028fn inheritable_in_guest_detlog_socket() -> Result<Option<std::os::fd::OwnedFd>, Error> {
2029    let sink = IN_GUEST_DETLOG_SINK.lock().unwrap();
2030    let Some(socket) = sink.as_ref() else {
2031        return Ok(None);
2032    };
2033    // SAFETY: F_DUPFD on an open descriptor returns a new descriptor or -1.
2034    let duplicate = unsafe { libc::fcntl(socket.as_raw_fd(), libc::F_DUPFD, 3) };
2035    if duplicate < 0 {
2036        return Err(Error::new(std::io::Error::last_os_error())
2037            .context("duplicating the in-guest DETLOG forwarding socket"));
2038    }
2039    // SAFETY: fcntl returned a new descriptor this function now owns.
2040    Ok(Some(unsafe {
2041        std::os::fd::OwnedFd::from_raw_fd(duplicate)
2042    }))
2043}
2044#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2045const SABRE_PATH_EVIDENCE_ENV: &str = "HERMIT_SABRE_PATH_EVIDENCE";
2046
2047/// The INFO policy for DETLOG records an in-guest Detcore (SaBRe's plugin,
2048/// in-guest LiteInst) forwards: the current subscriber's answer for every
2049/// target the CLI's `RUST_LOG` names (the text
2050/// `EffectiveFilter::from_default_env` builds every CLI subscriber from) and
2051/// for any other target.
2052#[cfg(any(feature = "sabre", feature = "liteinst"))]
2053fn in_guest_detlog_forward_policy() -> detcore::detlog::ForwardPolicy {
2054    let directives = std::env::var(tracing_subscriber::EnvFilter::DEFAULT_ENV).unwrap_or_default();
2055    detcore::detlog::ForwardPolicy::from_current_subscriber(&directives)
2056}
2057#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2058const SABRE_STAGING_DIRECTORY: &str = "/dev/shm";
2059
2060#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2061struct StagedSabreProgram {
2062    path: PathBuf,
2063}
2064
2065#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2066impl Drop for StagedSabreProgram {
2067    fn drop(&mut self) {
2068        let _ = fs::remove_file(&self.path);
2069    }
2070}
2071
2072#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2073fn sabre_program_needs_neutral_name(program: &Path) -> bool {
2074    program
2075        .file_name()
2076        .is_some_and(|name| name.as_bytes().starts_with(b"ld"))
2077}
2078
2079// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-845): Review the neutral-name workaround for SaBRe's
2080// dynamic-loader prefix collision.
2081#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2082fn stage_sabre_program_in(
2083    program: &Path,
2084    staging_directory: &Path,
2085) -> Result<Option<StagedSabreProgram>, Error> {
2086    if !sabre_program_needs_neutral_name(program) {
2087        return Ok(None);
2088    }
2089
2090    let path = staging_directory.join(format!("hermit-sabre-program-{}", std::process::id()));
2091    let mut source = fs::File::open(program).map_err(|error| {
2092        anyhow!(
2093            "failed to open SaBRe guest executable {} for neutral-name staging: {error}",
2094            program.display()
2095        )
2096    })?;
2097    let mut staged = fs::OpenOptions::new()
2098        .write(true)
2099        .create_new(true)
2100        .mode(0o700)
2101        .open(&path)
2102        .map_err(|error| {
2103            anyhow!(
2104                "failed to stage SaBRe guest executable {} as {}: {error}",
2105                program.display(),
2106                path.display()
2107            )
2108        })?;
2109    if let Err(error) = io::copy(&mut source, &mut staged) {
2110        let _ = fs::remove_file(&path);
2111        return Err(anyhow!(
2112            "failed to stage SaBRe guest executable {} as {}: {error}",
2113            program.display(),
2114            path.display()
2115        ));
2116    }
2117    drop(staged);
2118
2119    Ok(Some(StagedSabreProgram { path }))
2120}

2122// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-738): Review controller/plugin artifact separation.
2123#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2124fn sabre_runtime_library_path() -> io::Result<PathBuf> {
2125    if let Some(path) = hermit_resources::resource("libdetcore_sabre.so")?
2126        && path.is_file()
2127    {
2128        return Ok(path);
2129    }
2130
2131    let executable = std::env::current_exe()?;
2132    let directory = executable.parent().ok_or_else(|| {
2133        io::Error::new(
2134            io::ErrorKind::NotFound,
2135            "Hermit executable has no parent directory",
2136        )
2137    })?;
2138    [
2139        directory.join("libdetcore_sabre.so"),
2140        directory.join("deps/libdetcore_sabre.so"),
2141    ]
2142    .into_iter()
2143    .find(|path| path.is_file())
2144    .ok_or_else(|| {
2145        io::Error::new(
2146            io::ErrorKind::NotFound,
2147            format!(
2148                "libdetcore_sabre.so was not built beside {}",
2149                executable.display()
2150            ),
2151        )
2152    })
2153}
2154
2155#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2156fn sabre_runtime_unavailable_reason() -> Option<String> {
2157    if let Err(error) = resolve_sabre_binary() {
2158        return Some(error.to_string());
2159    }
2160    sabre_runtime_library_path().err().map(|error| {
2161        format!(
2162            "the Detcore SaBRe plugin is unavailable: {error}; build detcore-sabre and hermit in the same target directory"
2163        )
2164    })
2165}

2167// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2168// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-774): Review the bounded SaBRe RPC disconnect drain.
2169#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2170const SABRE_RPC_DISCONNECT_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(1);
2171
2172#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2173async fn wait_for_sabre_rpc_disconnects<T>(
2174    global: &Arc<T>,
2175    timeout: Duration,
2176) -> Result<(), usize> {
2177    let disconnected = tokio::time::timeout(timeout, async {
2178        while Arc::strong_count(global) > 1 {
2179            tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(1)).await;
2180        }
2181    })
2182    .await;
2183
2184    if disconnected.is_ok() {
2185        return Ok(());
2186    }
2187
2188    let live_references = Arc::strong_count(global).saturating_sub(1);
2189    if live_references == 0 {
2190        Ok(())
2191    } else {
2192        Err(live_references)
2193    }
2194}
2195
2196// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2197// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-782): Review SaBRe RPC server shutdown errors.
2198#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2199async fn stop_sabre_rpc_server<E>(
2200    server_task: tokio::task::JoinHandle<Result<(), E>>,
2201) -> Result<(), Error>
2202where
2203    E: std::fmt::Display,
2204{
2205    server_task.abort();
2206    match server_task.await {
2207        Err(error) if error.is_cancelled() => Ok(()),
2208        Err(error) => Err(anyhow!("SaBRe coordinator task failed: {error}")),
2209        Ok(Err(error)) => Err(anyhow!("SaBRe coordinator server failed: {error}")),
2210        Ok(Ok(())) => Err(anyhow!("SaBRe coordinator server stopped unexpectedly")),
2211    }
2212}
2213
2214// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2215// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-789): Review complete SaBRe RPC shutdown ordering.
2216#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2217async fn shutdown_sabre_rpc<T, E>(
2218    server_task: tokio::task::JoinHandle<Result<(), E>>,
2219    global: &Arc<T>,
2220    timeout: Duration,
2221) -> Result<(), Error>
2222where
2223    E: std::fmt::Display,
2224{
2225    let server_result = stop_sabre_rpc_server(server_task).await;
2226    let disconnect_result = wait_for_sabre_rpc_disconnects(global, timeout).await;
2227
2228    server_result?;
2229    disconnect_result.map_err(|live_references| {
2230        anyhow!("SaBRe coordinator stopped with {live_references} live RPC reference(s)")
2231    })
2232}
2233
2234fn ensure_backend_dispatch(backend: Backend) -> Result<(), Error> {
2235    // The CLI probes ptrace readiness before entering its container; repeating
2236    // the namespace probe here would test nested namespaces instead of the host.
2237    if backend == Backend::Ptrace {
2238        return Ok(());
2239    }
2240    if backend == Backend::E9patch {
2241        return Err(anyhow!(
2242            "backend `e9patch` requires CLI preprocessing; library callers must use \
2243             e9patch::prepare and then select `ptrace`"
2244        ));
2245    }
2246    // KVM and DBT have dedicated dispatches (`run_kvm` and `run_dbt`); neither
2247    // must reach this generic rejection path.
2248    backend.ensure_available()?;
2249    Err(anyhow!(
2250        "backend `{}` has no Hermit dispatch implementation",
2251        backend.as_str()
2252    ))
2253}
2254
2255/// Run one command with the Detcore tool executing inside a SaBRe plugin and
2256/// the single GlobalState held by this Hermit coordinator process.
2257// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2258// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-738): Review SaBRe coordinator lifetime and artifact loading.
2259#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2260async fn run_sabre(
2261    mut command: Command,
2262    config: DetConfig,
2263    print_summary: bool,
2264    print_summary_to_json_file: &Option<PathBuf>,
2265    capture_output: bool,
2266) -> Result<Output, Error> {
2267    let path_evidence_file = std::env::var_os(SABRE_PATH_EVIDENCE_ENV).map(PathBuf::from);
2268    // Whether this run passes the plugin a DETLOG socket (below), in which case
2269    // every Tool image must forward on it; see
2270    // detcore::Config::in_guest_detlog_forward_policy.
2271    let mut config = config;
2272    let policy = in_guest_detlog_forward_policy();
2273    config.in_guest_detlog_forward_policy = (policy.forwards_any()
2274        && IN_GUEST_DETLOG_SINK.lock().unwrap().is_some())
2275    .then(|| policy.encode());
2276    let sabre = resolve_sabre_binary()?;
2277    let plugin = sabre_runtime_library_path()
2278        .map_err(|error| anyhow!("failed to locate the Detcore SaBRe plugin: {error}"))?;
2279    let program = command.find_program().map_err(|error| {
2280        anyhow!(
2281            "failed to resolve SaBRe guest executable {:?}: {error}",
2282            command.get_program()
2283        )
2284    })?;
2285    let staged_program = stage_sabre_program_in(&program, Path::new(SABRE_STAGING_DIRECTORY))?;
2286    let launch_program = staged_program
2287        .as_ref()
2288        .map_or(program.as_path(), |staged| staged.path.as_path());
2289
2290    let bootstrap_launch =
2291        sabre_bootstrap::Launch::new(Path::new(&sabre), launch_program, &config)?;
2292
2293    // This runs after Hermit enters the guest mount namespace, where `/tmp`
2294    // names the container-visible temporary filesystem. Do not inherit a
2295    // host-side nested TMPDIR: validation commonly sets TMPDIR=/tmp/<run>/tmp,
2296    // but that parent path is hidden once the private `/tmp` mount is active.
2297    // The SaBRe plugin and coordinator must name the socket through the same
2298    // namespace-visible path.
2299    let socket_dir = tempfile::Builder::new()
2300        .prefix("hermit-sabre-rpc-")
2301        .tempdir_in(Path::new("/tmp"))?;
2302    let socket_path = socket_dir.path().join("coordinator.sock");
2303    let fallback_ready = Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false));
2304    let global = Arc::new(detcore::GlobalState::init_global_state(&config).await);
2305    let server = reverie_rpc_transport::RpcServer::bind_with_readiness(
2306        &socket_path,
2307        global.clone(),
2308        config.clone(),
2309        fallback_ready.clone(),
2310    )
2311    .map_err(|error| anyhow!("failed to start SaBRe coordinator RPC: {error}"))?;
2312    let server_task = tokio::spawn(async move { server.serve().await });
2313
2314    command.prepend_args([
2315        plugin.as_os_str(),
2316        OsStr::new("--"),
2317        launch_program.as_os_str(),
2318    ]);
2319    command.program(&sabre);
2320    command.env(SABRE_RPC_SOCKET_ENV, &socket_path);
2321    command.env_remove(sabre_bootstrap::ENVIRONMENT);
2322    if bootstrap_launch.initializes_random() {
2323        command.env(sabre_bootstrap::ENVIRONMENT, "1");
2324    }
2325    // Publish the configuration and clock RPC fingerprint for this coordinator.
2326    // The plugin is a separate artifact in the same target directory, so it can
2327    // be stale without looking it. Its check before connecting names the mismatch
2328    // instead of failing to decode Config or a later DetTime request field.
2329    // See detcore_model::config_wire_fingerprint.
2330    command.env(
2331        detcore::CONFIG_FINGERPRINT_ENV,
2332        detcore::config_wire_fingerprint(),
2333    );
2334    command.env_remove(SABRE_PATH_EVIDENCE_ENV);
2335    command.env_remove(SABRE_DETLOG_FORWARD_ENV);
2336    // Per emitting module, as each in-process `detlog!` callsite asks tracing:
2337    // asking only about `detcore` dropped every record a target-scoped
2338    // RUST_LOG (warn,detcore::random=info) keeps under ptrace.
2339    command.env_remove(SABRE_TOOL_OUTPUT_ENV);
2340    let detlog_policy = in_guest_detlog_forward_policy();
2341    // Kept open until the guest has started (it inherits its own copy).
2342    let mut _detlog_descriptor = None;
2343    if detlog_policy.forwards_any() {
2344        command.env(SABRE_DETLOG_FORWARD_ENV, detlog_policy.encode());
2345        // With a socket (`hermit run --verify`), the plugin sends each record
2346        // on it from a descriptor reverie-sabre protects from the guest, and
2347        // the coordinator writes each thread's records into the log at that
2348        // thread's scheduler turns, as for in-guest LiteInst. Without one it
2349        // writes them to the guest's standard error.
2350        if let Some(socket) = inheritable_in_guest_detlog_socket()? {
2351            command.env(
2352                SABRE_TOOL_OUTPUT_ENV,
2353                detcore::detlog::tool_output_env_value(socket.as_raw_fd())?,
2354            );
2355            _detlog_descriptor = Some(socket);
2356        }
2357    }
2358    command.env_remove("SABRE_BINARY");
2359    command.env_remove("SABRE_PLUGIN");
2360    // Always collect: the statistics page is a descriptor the guest can see,
2361    // so creating it only when a summary or DEBUG log is requested would make
2362    // those flags change the guest's view. The plugin takes the variable out
2363    // of the guest environment, and the descriptor sits above the plugin's
2364    // fixed RPC socket.
2365    let stats = reverie_sabre_stats::SabreStats::create(reverie::BackendStatsRequest::ENABLED)
2366        .map_err(|error| anyhow!("failed to create the SaBRe statistics page: {error}"))?
2367        .expect("an enabled request creates the statistics page");
2368    command.env(
2369        reverie_sabre_stats::BACKEND_STATS_ENV,
2370        stats.raw_fd().to_string(),
2371    );
2372
2373    // THE SOCKET PATH IS DELIBERATELY RANDOM AND MUST NOT BE COMPARED.
2374    //
2375    // `tempfile` appends a random 6-character suffix to the directory above, so
2376    // the absolute path differs on every launch by design -- that is what keeps
2377    // concurrent hermit invocations on a shared host from binding the same
2378    // coordinator socket. Emitting it at INFO put per-launch randomness into the
2379    // INFO stream, which `--verify-strict` compares:
2380    //
2381    //   Mismatch at log messages 2 (run 1) and 2 (run 2):
2382    //   < socket=/tmp/hermit-sabre-rpc-Gw6tUi/coordinator.sock
2383    //   > socket=/tmp/hermit-sabre-rpc-7SZ94p/coordinator.sock
2384    //
2385    // That fails EVERY SaBRe verify cell before the guest executes an
2386    // instruction, for a host-side value with no guest-visible content. The
2387    // defect was in the oracle, not the backend.
2388    //
2389    // WHY THE VALUE IS DROPPED FROM INFO RATHER THAN MADE DETERMINISTIC: the two
2390    // `--verify` runs execute in SEPARATE CHILD PROCESSES, so any path stable
2391    // across both would have to be derived from state shared between them. That
2392    // trades `tempfile`'s guaranteed-fresh directory for a reused one, and a
2393    // run1 that dies holding the socket would then make run2 fail to bind -- a
2394    // real new failure mode in exchange for a cosmetic field. The path keeps its
2395    // randomness; only the compared stream stops carrying it.
2396    //
2397    // This is NOT a comparator relaxation. No normalization envelope is widened,
2398    // so any future randomness leaking into this banner still fails the
2399    // comparison. The full path remains available one level down.
2400    tracing::debug!(
2401        target: "hermit::sabre",
2402        socket = %socket_path.display(),
2403        "SaBRe coordinator RPC socket (per-launch temp path, deliberately not at INFO)",
2404    );
2405    tracing::info!(
2406        target: "hermit::sabre",
2407        guest = %program.display(),
2408        plugin = %plugin.display(),
2409        "launching Detcore guest through SaBRe with coordinator RPC",
2410    );
2411
2412    let supervised = match sabre_ptrace::run(
2413        command.into_std_lossy(),
2414        PathBuf::from(&sabre),
2415        plugin.clone(),
2416        fallback_ready,
2417        global.clone(),
2418        bootstrap_launch,
2419        stats.clone(),
2420        capture_output,
2421    )
2422    .await
2423    {
2424        Ok(supervised) => supervised,
2425        Err(error) => {
2426            global.release_all_physical_process_exits();
2427            global.force_shutdown_with_error();
2428            let _ = shutdown_sabre_rpc(server_task, &global, SABRE_RPC_DISCONNECT_TIMEOUT).await;
2429            return Err(error);
2430        }
2431    };
2432    // The supervisor returns only after every tracee reached a final kernel wait status. Release
2433    // the root process's barrier (and any intentionally unreaped child barriers) before scheduler
2434    // shutdown; no guest thread remains that could race timer fast-forward here.
2435    global.release_all_physical_process_exits();
2436    // A SaBRe execution in which no guest thread ever reached the coordinator
2437    // is an execution in which Detcore was never loaded as a Reverie tool: the
2438    // guest ran on bare Linux. Treat it exactly like a failed run so the
2439    // scheduler is torn down here instead of blocking forever in `clean_up`
2440    // below, waiting for a guest thread that will never register.
2441    let detcore_never_engaged = !supervised.path_evidence.guest_rpc_observed;
2442    let requires_forced_shutdown = !supervised.status.success() || detcore_never_engaged;
2443    if requires_forced_shutdown {
2444        global.force_shutdown_with_error();
2445    }
2446    // Emit one unconditional, machine-readable per-run fact instead of a
2447    // free-form warning. SaBRe loads the guest interpreter before the Detcore
2448    // plugin, so pre-plugin loader syscalls are structurally absent from its
2449    // observation envelope. Versioning that fact lets scorecard consumers
2450    // reject old or incomplete records rather than infer coverage from silence.
2451    //
2452    // Keep this in the controller-diagnostic stream rather than writing
2453    // directly to the process stderr that also carries captured guest stderr.
2454    // WARN is Hermit's default tracing level, and `--log-file` can therefore
2455    // separate the unconditional controller fact from guest output without a
2456    // comparator exception. A missing record remains distinct from a record
2457    // whose counters are zero.
2458    let backend_evidence = sabre_backend_evidence_line(&supervised.path_evidence);
2459    tracing::warn!(target: "hermit::sabre", "{backend_evidence}");
2460    if let Some(path) = path_evidence_file {
2461        let mut file = fs::OpenOptions::new()
2462            .create(true)
2463            .append(true)
2464            .mode(0o600)
2465            .open(&path)
2466            .map_err(|error| {
2467                anyhow!(
2468                    "failed to open SaBRe path evidence {}: {error}",
2469                    path.display()
2470                )
2471            })?;
2472        serde_json::to_writer(&mut file, &supervised.path_evidence)?;
2473        writeln!(file)?;
2474    }
2475    let output = Output {
2476        status: supervised.status.into(),
2477        stdout: supervised.stdout,
2478        stderr: supervised.stderr,
2479    };
2480
2481    shutdown_sabre_rpc(server_task, &global, SABRE_RPC_DISCONNECT_TIMEOUT).await?;
2482    let mut global = Arc::try_unwrap(global).map_err(|global| {
2483        anyhow!(
2484            "SaBRe coordinator stopped with {} live RPC reference(s)",
2485            Arc::strong_count(&global) - 1
2486        )
2487    })?;
2488    if requires_forced_shutdown {
2489        global.cancel_internal_scheduler().await;
2490    }
2491    let dispatch_stats = backend_stats::report(
2492        Backend::Sabre,
2493        backend_stats::request(print_summary_to_json_file),
2494        &stats,
2495    );
2496    global
2497        .clean_up_with_dispatch_stats(print_summary, print_summary_to_json_file, dispatch_stats)
2498        .await;
2499    tracing::info!(
2500        target: "hermit::sabre::fallback",
2501        ptrace_fallback_sites = supervised.path_evidence.ptrace_fallback_sites,
2502        trusted_shared_object_sites = supervised.path_evidence.trusted_shared_object_sites,
2503        guest_rpc_observed = supervised.path_evidence.guest_rpc_observed,
2504        "SaBRe ptrace fallback completed",
2505    );
2506    if detcore_never_engaged {
2507        return Err(anyhow!(
2508            "{}",
2509            sabre_uninstrumented_guest_message(&output.status)
2510        ));
2511    }
2512    Ok(output)
2513}
2514
2515#[cfg(not(feature = "sabre"))]
2516async fn run_sabre(
2517    _command: Command,
2518    _config: DetConfig,
2519    _print_summary: bool,
2520    _print_summary_to_json_file: &Option<PathBuf>,
2521    _capture_output: bool,
2522) -> Result<Output, Error> {
2523    Backend::Sabre.ensure_available()?;
2524    unreachable!("SaBRe availability must fail when the feature is disabled");
2525}
2526
2527/// Classify the existing SaBRe reach evidence without collapsing "never
2528/// engaged" into the same zero-valued state as a fully exercised run.
2529#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2530fn sabre_reach_state(guest_rpc_observed: bool, ptrace_fallback_sites: usize) -> &'static str {
2531    match (guest_rpc_observed, ptrace_fallback_sites) {
2532        (false, _) => "no-detcore-reached",
2533        (true, 1..) => "degraded-ptrace-fallback",
2534        (true, 0) => "sabre-exercised",
2535    }
2536}
2537
2538/// Produce the versioned SaBRe backend fact consumed by compatibility reports.
2539///
2540/// `preplugin_coverage=absent` is a property of SaBRe's launch order: the guest
2541/// interpreter runs before the Detcore plugin is loaded. It is recorded rather
2542/// than inferred from counters because the ptrace fallback cannot observe that
2543/// interval either.
2544///
2545/// Preserve the originating #1725 measurement as provenance for that field:
2546/// on its measurement host, `/bin/true` produced 3 COMMITs under SaBRe versus
2547/// 14 under ptrace; the 11 absent records were the loader's libc.so.6 path
2548/// resolution. The launch-order fact, not that host-specific count, is the
2549/// runtime contract.
2550#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2551fn sabre_backend_evidence_line(evidence: &sabre_ptrace::PathEvidence) -> String {
2552    format!(
2553        ":: Backend: sabre static rewriting + ptrace runtime; run_mode=run; \
2554         evidence_schema={}; preplugin_coverage=absent; ptrace_fallback_sites={}; \
2555         trusted_shared_object_sites={}; guest_rpc_observed={}; reach_state={}",
2556        evidence.schema,
2557        evidence.ptrace_fallback_sites,
2558        evidence.trusted_shared_object_sites,
2559        evidence.guest_rpc_observed,
2560        sabre_reach_state(evidence.guest_rpc_observed, evidence.ptrace_fallback_sites),
2561    )
2562}
2563
2564/// Explain a SaBRe run whose guest never reached the Detcore coordinator.
2565///
2566/// `guest_rpc_observed` is set by the coordinator RPC listener the first time a
2567/// SaBRe-loaded guest connects. When it stays false the guest completed without
2568/// Detcore intercepting anything, so the run carries no determinism guarantee
2569/// whatsoever -- its timing, scheduling, PIDs, and clock reads all came from the
2570/// host. Reporting that as a successful `hermit run` would be a fail-open
2571/// determinism hole, so the caller turns it into a hard error and this function
2572/// supplies the diagnosis.
2573///
2574/// The dominant cause is a guest whose syscall sites SaBRe never rewrote. A
2575/// statically linked ELF is the sharp edge: it has no dynamic loader and no
2576/// shared library through which SaBRe could regain control, so an unrewritten
2577/// static client runs entirely on bare Linux with no second chance.
2578#[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
2579fn sabre_uninstrumented_guest_message(status: &ExitStatus) -> String {
2580    format!(
2581        "the SaBRe backend finished ({status:?}) without the guest ever reaching the Detcore \
2582         coordinator: no syscall was intercepted, so this run applied no determinization at all \
2583         and its result is not a Hermit guarantee. This means SaBRe rewrote no syscall site in \
2584         the guest -- most often a statically linked ELF, whose syscall sites SaBRe must patch \
2585         in the client image itself because there is no dynamic loader to intercept."
2586    )
2587}
2588
2589/// Guest-physical memory available to the single-process KVM personality.
2590// The KVM personality is a sparse MAP_NORESERVE address space. QEMU needs room
2591// for its own ELF mappings in addition to the nested machine's RAM mapping.
2592const KVM_GUEST_MEMORY_BYTES: usize = 1024 * 1024 * 1024;
2593
2594/// Maximum `#!` interpreter indirection levels, matching the Linux kernel's
2595/// `BINPRM_MAX_RECURSION` limit for chained script interpreters.
2596const MAX_SHEBANG_DEPTH: usize = 4;
2597
2598/// Resolve `#!` interpreter scripts before the reverie-kvm ELF loader runs.
2599///
2600/// The KVM ELF loader can only map ELF images, so a guest program that is
2601/// actually a `#!`-script (for example `/usr/local/bin/file` -> `#!/bin/bash`,
2602/// or `/usr/bin/pkg-config` -> `#!/usr/bin/sh`) must be rewritten to launch its
2603/// interpreter, exactly as the kernel's `execve(2)` `binfmt_script` handler
2604/// does. On success the returned image is an ELF and `argv` has the interpreter,
2605/// its shebang arguments, and the script path prepended in kernel order:
2606/// `[interp, shebang_args.., script_path, <original argv[1..]>]`.
2607///
2608/// The interpreter line is parsed with hermit's shared [`Shebang`] so the KVM
2609/// backend matches how the ptrace backend and recorder treat `#!`-scripts.
2610fn resolve_kvm_shebang(
2611    resolved_program: &Path,
2612    mut argv: Vec<String>,
2613) -> Result<(PathBuf, Vec<String>, Vec<u8>), Error> {
2614    let mut load_path = resolved_program.to_path_buf();
2615    let mut image = fs::read(&load_path)
2616        .map_err(|error| anyhow!("failed to read KVM guest executable {load_path:?}: {error}"))?;
2617
2618    let mut depth = 0;
2619    while image.starts_with(b"#!") {
2620        depth += 1;
2621        if depth > MAX_SHEBANG_DEPTH {
2622            return Err(anyhow!(
2623                "too many levels of `#!` interpreter indirection loading {resolved_program:?}"
2624            ));
2625        }
2626        let (interpreter, shebang_args) = Shebang::from_buf(&image)
2627            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("malformed `#!` interpreter line in {load_path:?}"))?
2628            .into_parts();
2629        let interpreter_str = interpreter
2630            .to_str()
2631            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("non-UTF-8 `#!` interpreter path in {load_path:?}"))?
2632            .to_owned();
2633
2634        // Rewrite argv in kernel order. The prior argv[0] (the script's own
2635        // name) is dropped on the first level; on deeper levels the previous
2636        // interpreter path is preserved as a positional argument, matching
2637        // `binfmt_script`.
2638        let mut rewritten = Vec::with_capacity(argv.len() + shebang_args.len() + 2);
2639        rewritten.push(interpreter_str);
2640        for arg in &shebang_args {
2641            rewritten.push(
2642                arg.to_str()
2643                    .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("non-UTF-8 `#!` interpreter argument in {load_path:?}"))?
2644                    .to_owned(),
2645            );
2646        }
2647        rewritten.push(load_path.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
2648        rewritten.extend_from_slice(&argv[1..]);
2649        argv = rewritten;
2650
2651        load_path = interpreter;
2652        image = fs::read(&load_path).map_err(|error| {
2653            anyhow!(
2654                "failed to read `#!` interpreter {load_path:?} for {resolved_program:?}: {error}"
2655            )
2656        })?;
2657    }
2658
2659    Ok((load_path, argv, image))
2660}
2661
2662/// The container replaces this host directory with a private, empty tmpfs, so
2663/// nothing beneath it is visible to the guest. Kept next to the diagnostic that
2664/// depends on it; `run.rs` has its own `TMP_DIR` for the mount itself.
2665const CONTAINER_REPLACED_TMP: &str = "/tmp";
2666
2667/// Explain why a working directory that plainly exists on the host could not be
2668/// resolved for the guest.
2669///
2670/// ⚠️ THIS FUNCTION EXISTS BECAUSE THE BARE ERROR CAUSED A MISDIAGNOSIS, not to
2671/// be tidy. `fs::canonicalize` runs after the container's mounts are applied, and
2672/// the container bind-mounts a fresh empty tmpfs over `/tmp`
2673/// (`bin/hermit/container.rs`). So a cwd beneath `/tmp` is genuinely absent from
2674/// the guest's mount namespace and `canonicalize` returns a perfectly correct
2675/// `NotFound` -- for a directory the user can `ls`.
2676///
2677/// Reported 2026-08-25 as "run_kvm_ cli tests fail from a git worktree". They do
2678/// not. Measured across five working directories with one binary: a PLAIN
2679/// directory under `/tmp` with no git in it FAILS, and a linked git worktree
2680/// under `/home` WORKS. The only property that tracks the failure is "the cwd is
2681/// under /tmp"; `/tmp` itself resolves because the mount POINT exists. The git
2682/// hypothesis cost real investigation time, and it came from this message saying
2683/// only "No such file or directory".
2684///
2685/// It mattered more than a stray report because the historical landing recipe
2686/// placed detached worktrees below `/tmp`, exactly where this fires. Current
2687/// dev-hermit worktrees are created through wrkslots below `worktrees/`, but the
2688/// diagnostic remains necessary for arbitrary callers and older checkouts.
2689///
2690/// The message names the mechanism and both ways out. It deliberately does NOT
2691/// silently fall back to `/`: changing the guest's working directory out from
2692/// under a caller who asked for a specific one would trade a loud, correct
2693/// failure for a quiet, wrong success.
2694fn kvm_cwd_resolution_error(requested_cwd: &Path, error: &std::io::Error) -> Error {
2695    let under_replaced_tmp = requested_cwd.starts_with(CONTAINER_REPLACED_TMP)
2696        && requested_cwd != Path::new(CONTAINER_REPLACED_TMP);
2697    if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound && under_replaced_tmp {
2698        anyhow!(
2699            "failed to resolve KVM guest working directory {:?}: {error}\n\
2700             \n\
2701             This directory may well exist on the host. The container replaces \
2702             {CONTAINER_REPLACED_TMP} with a private, empty tmpfs, so nothing beneath \
2703             {CONTAINER_REPLACED_TMP} is visible to the guest, and resolving the working \
2704             directory happens inside that mount namespace.\n\
2705             Either run from a directory outside {CONTAINER_REPLACED_TMP}, or pass \
2706             --workdir with a path that exists inside the guest.",
2707            requested_cwd
2708        )
2709    } else {
2710        anyhow!(
2711            "failed to resolve KVM guest working directory {:?}: {error}",
2712            requested_cwd
2713        )
2714    }
2715}
2716
2717/// Bind KVM's raw mountinfo and fdinfo to the same filesystem namespace.
2718///
2719/// The CLI supplies IdentityGuard provenance from the completed container.
2720/// Recapture that full row/parent order before ELF installation, which snapshots
2721/// the same namespace in the backend, and refuse stale producer provenance.
2722/// Library and no-namespace callers without provenance retain this capture.
2723/// KVM does not permit guest namespace changes. Later guest-visible mountinfo
2724/// views still use Detcore's existing ordered-subset validation.
2725fn prepare_kvm_mountinfo_config(
2726    config: &mut DetConfig,
2727    capture: impl FnOnce() -> Result<Vec<u64>, Error>,
2728) -> Result<(), Error> {
2729    if !config.mountinfo_mount_ids_captured
2730        && (!config.mountinfo_mount_ids.is_empty() || !config.mount_id_assignment_order.is_empty())
2731    {
2732        anyhow::bail!("KVM mount identities have no producer-captured namespace");
2733    }
2734    let order = capture().context("failed to capture the KVM backend mount namespace")?;
2735    if config.mountinfo_mount_ids_captured {
2736        if config.mountinfo_mount_ids != order {
2737            anyhow::bail!(
2738                "KVM producer-captured mount identities do not match the backend namespace"
2739            );
2740        }
2741    } else {
2742        config.mountinfo_mount_ids = order;
2743        config.mountinfo_mount_ids_captured = true;
2744    }
2745    // The backend now exposes the actual namespace's devices. There is no
2746    // synthetic 0:1 root device to translate; stat and mountinfo use the same
2747    // raw identities before entering Detcore's shared DevicePool.
2748    config.mountinfo_device_rewrites.clear();
2749    Ok(())
2750}
2751
2752// AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2753// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(https://github.com/rrnewton/hermit/issues/3695): Review
2754// host metadata timestamps for KVM runs.
2755/// Whether a KVM run asks the backend for the timestamps the kernel stored
2756/// rather than its fixed value.
2757///
2758/// With virtual metadata, Detcore rewrites every timestamp that a stat call
2759/// returns to the guest, and it reads the stored host values for its own
2760/// checks: `set_file_times` confirms that `utimensat` stored the requested
2761/// mtime. KVM replaces those timestamps with a fixed value unless the run asks
2762/// for the stored ones. The backend refuses this setting unless Detcore owns
2763/// every guest thread, which `run_kvm` never opts out of. The stored values sit
2764/// in the guest's buffer until Detcore rewrites them, so the run must also
2765/// sequentialize threads: then no other guest thread runs in that interval.
2766fn kvm_reports_stored_metadata_timestamps(config: &DetConfig) -> bool {
2767    config.virtualize_metadata && config.sequentialize_threads
2768}
2769
2770/// Dispatch a command onto the real reverie-kvm Tool runtime.
2771async fn run_kvm(
2772    command: &Command,
2773    mut config: DetConfig,
2774    print_summary: bool,
2775    print_summary_to_json_file: &Option<PathBuf>,
2776    capture_output: bool,
2777) -> Result<Output, Error> {
2778    let dispatch_started = Instant::now();
2779    prepare_kvm_mountinfo_config(&mut config, capture_mountinfo_identity_order)?;
2780    let stdin = if capture_output {
2781        let (snapshot_reserved, snapshot) = output_backend_stdin_reservation()?;
2782        if snapshot_reserved {
2783            snapshot
2784        } else {
2785            // Public KVM output-capture callers do not pass through the CLI's
2786            // verify setup. Preserve their existing stdin behavior instead of
2787            // silently replacing it with /dev/null merely because output is
2788            // captured.
2789            reserved_kvm_stdin()?
2790        }
2791    } else {
2792        reserved_kvm_stdin()?
2793    };
2794    let requested_cwd = command
2795        .get_current_dir()
2796        .map(Path::to_owned)
2797        .unwrap_or(std::env::current_dir()?);
2798    let cwd = fs::canonicalize(&requested_cwd)
2799        .map_err(|error| kvm_cwd_resolution_error(&requested_cwd, &error))?;
2800    let program = command
2801        .get_program()
2802        .to_str()
2803        .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("KVM guest executable path is not valid UTF-8"))?
2804        .to_owned();
2805    if !cwd.is_dir() {
2806        return Err(anyhow!(
2807            "KVM guest working directory is not a directory: {:?}",
2808            cwd
2809        ));
2810    }
2811    let resolved_program = command.find_program().map_err(|error| {
2812        anyhow!("failed to resolve KVM guest executable {program:?} in the guest PATH: {error}")
2813    })?;
2814    let mut argv = Vec::with_capacity(1 + command.get_args().count());
2815    argv.push(program.clone());
2816    for argument in command.get_args() {
2817        argv.push(
2818            argument
2819                .to_str()
2820                .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("KVM guest argument is not valid UTF-8"))?
2821                .to_owned(),
2822        );
2823    }
2824
2825    // Rewrite `#!`-scripts to their interpreter before the ELF loader sees them.
2826    let (_interpreter_path, argv, image) = resolve_kvm_shebang(&resolved_program, argv)?;
2827    // After shebang resolution the executable is the interpreter (argv[0]).
2828    let program = argv.first().cloned().unwrap_or(program);
2829    let envp = command
2830        .get_captured_envs()
2831        .into_iter()
2832        .map(|(key, value)| {
2833            let key = key
2834                .to_str()
2835                .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("KVM guest environment key is not valid UTF-8"))?;
2836            let value = value
2837                .to_str()
2838                .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!("KVM guest environment value is not valid UTF-8"))?;
2839            Ok(format!("{key}={value}"))
2840        })
2841        .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, Error>>()?;
2842    tracing::info!(
2843        target: "hermit::kvm",
2844        program = %program,
2845        argv = ?argv,
2846        cwd = %cwd.display(),
2847        env_count = envp.len(),
2848        "launching guest through reverie-kvm",
2849    );
2850    let argv = argv.iter().map(String::as_str).collect::<Vec<_>>();
2851    let envp = envp.iter().map(String::as_str).collect::<Vec<_>>();
2852
2853    let setup_started = Instant::now();
2854    // `prepare_backend_config` already copied `KvmBackend::capabilities()`,
2855    // which reports the backend's own CPUID table and that, of the madvise
2856    // advice with guest-visible semantics, it implements only MADV_DONTNEED.
2857    // KVM does not enter Hermit's UTS namespace, so Detcore must provide the
2858    // same synthetic identity that the namespace-backed ptrace path exposes.
2859    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-998): Review KVM UTS namespace parity.
2860    config.has_uts_namespace = false;
2861    let random_seed = config.rng_seed();
2862    let mut backend = reverie_kvm::KvmBackend::new_with_stdin(KVM_GUEST_MEMORY_BYTES, stdin)
2863        .map_err(|error| anyhow!("failed to initialize reverie-kvm: {error}"))?;
2864    // AUTONOMOUS-BOT-IMPLEMENTED
2865    // TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-1120): Review KVM's canonical Detcore root identity.
2866    backend
2867        .set_root_pid(detcore::ROOT_DETPID.as_raw())
2868        .map_err(|error| anyhow!("failed to configure KVM root PID: {error}"))?;
2869    // Guests see their task IDs, so number them as the ptrace reference does
2870    // (one extra ID per task for its tracer thread; see reverie::task_ids).
2871    backend
2872        .set_ids_per_task(reverie::task_ids::HERMIT_PTRACE_IDS_PER_TASK)
2873        .map_err(|error| anyhow!("failed to configure KVM task IDs: {error}"))?;
2874    // The ptrace launcher execs the path `find_program` resolved, so that is
2875    // the filename Linux records in AT_EXECFN and copies to the top of the
2876    // stack, including for a `#!` script whose argv now starts with the
2877    // interpreter.
2878    backend
2879        .install_static_elf_with_exec_context(
2880            &image,
2881            resolved_program.as_os_str(),
2882            &argv,
2883            &envp,
2884            &cwd,
2885        )
2886        .map_err(|error| anyhow!("failed to load KVM guest executable {program:?}: {error}"))?;
2887    backend
2888        .set_random_seed(random_seed)
2889        .map_err(|error| anyhow!("failed to configure KVM guest random seed: {error}"))?;
2890    // The KVM backend defaults to Tool-owned guest threads: CLONE_THREAD
2891    // workers are driven through the Detcore tool loop and their futex/CLEARTID
2892    // synchronization routes to Detcore, matching the golden ptrace model
2893    // ("follow children"). Detcore registers every cloned thread as a tool
2894    // thread (detcore/src/syscalls/threads.rs), so the backend must stay in
2895    // that mode: host-owned threads would split worker execution and futex
2896    // ownership across two synchronization domains, which deadlocks
2897    // pthread_join.
2898    if kvm_reports_stored_metadata_timestamps(&config) {
2899        backend
2900            .set_host_metadata_timestamps(true)
2901            .map_err(|error| anyhow!("failed to configure KVM metadata timestamps: {error}"))?;
2902    }
2903
2904    let execution_started = Instant::now();
2905    let completion = backend
2906        .run_static_elf_with_tool_completion::<Detcore>(config, capture_output)
2907        .await
2908        .map_err(|error| kvm_execution_error(error, None))?;
2909    let cleanup_started = Instant::now();
2910    let result =
2911        finish_kvm_tool_completion(completion, print_summary, print_summary_to_json_file).await;
2912    let teardown_started = Instant::now();
2913    // Drop explicitly so the host's KVM VM teardown cost remains observable.
2914    drop(backend);
2915    let teardown_finished = Instant::now();
2916    let (code, stdout, stderr) = result?;
2917
2918    // Every field below is a host wall-clock duration. Keep this diagnostic at
2919    // DEBUG so it remains available for backend profiling without entering the
2920    // INFO stream exposed to canonical comparison. KVM's built-in verification
2921    // is still output/status-only; this classification does not claim KVM L2.
2922    tracing::debug!(
2923        target: "hermit::kvm",
2924        prepare_us = setup_started.duration_since(dispatch_started).as_micros() as u64,
2925        setup_us = execution_started.duration_since(setup_started).as_micros() as u64,
2926        execution_us = cleanup_started.duration_since(execution_started).as_micros() as u64,
2927        cleanup_us = teardown_started.duration_since(cleanup_started).as_micros() as u64,
2928        teardown_us = teardown_finished.duration_since(teardown_started).as_micros() as u64,
2929        lifecycle_us = teardown_finished.duration_since(dispatch_started).as_micros() as u64,
2930        "reverie-kvm lifecycle phase timings",
2931    );
2932
2933    if !capture_output {
2934        std::io::stdout().write_all(&stdout)?;
2935        std::io::stderr().write_all(&stderr)?;
2936    }
2937
2938    Ok(Output {
2939        status: ExitStatus::Exited(code),
2940        stdout,
2941        stderr,
2942    })
2943}
2944
2945// A runtime error still owns GlobalState. Finish its terminal daemon before
2946// returning the typed cause to the CLI's error classifier.
2947async fn finish_kvm_tool_completion(
2948    completion: reverie_kvm::ToolRunCompletion<detcore::GlobalState>,
2949    print_summary: bool,
2950    print_summary_to_json_file: &Option<PathBuf>,
2951) -> Result<(i32, Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>), Error> {
2952    match completion.result {
2953        Ok(output) => {
2954            completion
2955                .global_state
2956                .clean_up(print_summary, print_summary_to_json_file)
2957                .await;
2958            Ok(output)
2959        }
2960        Err(error) => {
2961            let cleanup = completion
2962                .global_state
2963                .clean_up_after_backend_failure()
2964                .await;
2965            Err(kvm_execution_error(error, Some(cleanup)))
2966        }
2967    }
2968}
2969
2970fn kvm_execution_error(
2971    primary: reverie_kvm::Error,
2972    cleanup: Option<detcore::BackendFailureCleanup>,
2973) -> Error {
2974    // The manifest runner retains the first Error line. Keep the cause there,
2975    // as well as the typed backend and optional scheduler errors in the chain.
2976    let message = format!("KVM guest execution failed: {primary}");
2977    let mut error = Error::new(primary);
2978    if let Some(cleanup) = cleanup {
2979        if let Err(recording) = cleanup.preemption_recording {
2980            error = error.context(format!("partial preemption recording failed: {recording}"));
2981        }
2982        if let Err(scheduler) = cleanup.scheduler {
2983            error = error.context(scheduler);
2984        }
2985    }
2986    error.context(message)
2987}

2995// TODO-HUMAN-REVIEW(PR-743): Review bounded relaunch before DBT guest execution.
2996#[cfg(feature = "dbt")]
2997fn dbt_client_thread_start_failed(status: &std::process::ExitStatus) -> bool {
2998    status.code() == Some(reverie_dbt::CLIENT_THREAD_START_FAILURE_EXIT_CODE)
2999}
3000
3001/// Keys that `HERMIT_DBT_DETCONFIG` still carries although [`DetConfig`] no
3002/// longer has the switches they named. Each entry is the key, its fixed value,
3003/// and the key it preceded in the old field order.
3004///
3005/// These are serialization constants. Nothing reads them back: the DBT runtime
3006/// decodes the variable with [`detcore::from_legacy_backend_json`], which
3007/// ignores keys the legacy form did not name. The values are the only ones the
3008/// deleted switches ever took on any backend.
3009const LEGACY_DBT_DETCONFIG_KEYS: [(&str, bool, &str); 3] = [
3010    (
3011        "use_thread_local_clock_reads",
3012        false,
3013        "detect_host_clock_futex_timeouts",
3014    ),
3015    (
3016        "backend_serializes_fork_children",
3017        false,
3018        "backend_tracks_process_children",
3019    ),
3020    (
3021        "backend_dispatches_thread_tools",
3022        true,
3023        "backend_tracks_process_children",
3024    ),
3025];
3026
3027/// Serialize `config` for the DBT runtime's `HERMIT_DBT_DETCONFIG` variable.
3028///
3029/// This is not plain `serde_json::to_string` because the variable sits in the
3030/// guest's own environment. A guest can read it through `getenv`, `printenv`
3031/// or `/proc/self/environ`, so its bytes are guest-visible. Two later changes
3032/// to [`DetConfig`] would otherwise have changed what every DBT guest
3033/// observes, and each is undone here:
3034///
3035/// - The backend facts moved into [`DetConfig::backend`].
3036///   [`detcore::to_legacy_backend_json`] writes them back out as the fifteen
3037///   separate keys they were, at the position the object carried them.
3038/// - Three always-constant switches were deleted, which would have shortened
3039///   the variable by 117 bytes. The keys in `LEGACY_DBT_DETCONFIG_KEYS` are
3040///   re-inserted at their old positions.
3041///
3042/// The output is byte-identical to what the same config produced before
3043/// either change. That is pinned by golden bytes in the tests below.
3044///
3045/// Remove this shim when the config reaches the DBT runtime through a private
3046/// channel that is stripped before the guest starts. SaBRe already works that
3047/// way: its plugin receives the config over the coordinator RPC handshake and
3048/// removes its own variables with `take_private_env` before guest code runs.
3049/// After that, the variable is no longer guest-visible and its bytes stop
3050/// mattering.
3051#[doc(hidden)]
3052pub fn dbt_detconfig_json(config: &DetConfig) -> serde_json::Result<String> {
3053    insert_legacy_dbt_detconfig_keys(&detcore::to_legacy_backend_json(config)?)
3054}
3055
3056/// Re-insert [`LEGACY_DBT_DETCONFIG_KEYS`] into a serialized [`DetConfig`].
3057///
3058/// The insertion works on the parsed top-level object, never on substrings,
3059/// so string values that happen to contain a key name cannot move it. It
3060/// refuses, rather than guessing, when an anchor key is missing or repeated or
3061/// when a legacy key is already present.
3062fn insert_legacy_dbt_detconfig_keys(serialized: &str) -> serde_json::Result<String> {
3063    use serde::de::MapAccess;
3064    use serde::ser::Error as _;
3065    use serde::ser::SerializeMap;
3066    use serde_json::value::RawValue;
3067
3068    /// The top-level members of a JSON object in document order, each value
3069    /// kept as its exact serialized bytes.
3070    struct Members<'a>(Vec<(&'a str, &'a RawValue)>);
3071
3072    impl<'de> serde::Deserialize<'de> for Members<'de> {
3073        fn deserialize<D: serde::Deserializer<'de>>(deserializer: D) -> Result<Self, D::Error> {
3074            struct MembersVisitor;
3075            impl<'de> serde::de::Visitor<'de> for MembersVisitor {
3076                type Value = Members<'de>;
3077                fn expecting(&self, formatter: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
3078                    formatter.write_str("a serialized Detcore config object")
3079                }
3080                fn visit_map<A: MapAccess<'de>>(self, mut map: A) -> Result<Self::Value, A::Error> {
3081                    let mut members = Vec::new();
3082                    while let Some(member) = map.next_entry()? {
3083                        members.push(member);
3084                    }
3085                    Ok(Members(members))
3086                }
3087            }
3088            deserializer.deserialize_map(MembersVisitor)
3089        }
3090    }
3091
3092    impl serde::Serialize for Members<'_> {
3093        fn serialize<S: serde::Serializer>(&self, serializer: S) -> Result<S::Ok, S::Error> {
3094            let mut map = serializer.serialize_map(None)?;
3095            for (key, value) in &self.0 {
3096                for (legacy_key, fixed_value, anchor) in LEGACY_DBT_DETCONFIG_KEYS {
3097                    if anchor == *key {
3098                        map.serialize_entry(legacy_key, &fixed_value)?;
3099                    }
3100                }
3101                map.serialize_entry(key, value)?;
3102            }
3103            map.end()
3104        }
3105    }
3106
3107    let members: Members<'_> = serde_json::from_str(serialized)?;
3108    for (legacy_key, _, anchor) in LEGACY_DBT_DETCONFIG_KEYS {
3109        let anchors = members.0.iter().filter(|(key, _)| *key == anchor).count();
3110        if anchors != 1 {
3111            return Err(serde_json::Error::custom(format!(
3112                "cannot place legacy DBT config key {legacy_key:?}: its anchor key {anchor:?} \
3113                 appears {anchors} times, expected exactly once"
3114            )));
3115        }
3116        if members.0.iter().any(|(key, _)| *key == legacy_key) {
3117            return Err(serde_json::Error::custom(format!(
3118                "legacy DBT config key {legacy_key:?} is already present; it must come only \
3119                 from the fixed legacy table"
3120            )));
3121        }
3122    }
3123    serde_json::to_string(&members)
3124}

3126#[cfg(test)]
3127mod dbt_detconfig_tests {
3128    use std::path::PathBuf;
3129
3130    use clap::Parser;
3131    use detcore_model::config::MountInfoRootRewrite;

3139    /// `HERMIT_DBT_DETCONFIG` bytes for each config below, captured with
3140    /// `serde_json::to_string` from a build of
3141    /// 806cf2fa38d13b806195d54aeba22d282d79f33d, which still had the legacy
3142    /// switches. Any later change to `DetConfig`'s serialized fields also
3143    /// changes this guest-visible variable, so this test is meant to fail on
3144    /// it and make that change a deliberate one.
3145    ///
3146    /// One deliberate change since that capture: the empty
3147    /// `fdinfo_unlisted_mount_ids` key was renamed `mount_id_assignment_order`
3148    /// when Detcore began numbering mount IDs in first-observation order
3149    /// (RECORD_VERSION 0x122). Every other byte is the 806cf2fa output.
3150    const GOLDEN: [(&str, &str); 3] = [
3151        (
3152            "default",
3153            include_str!("../tests/fixtures/dbt/detconfig-806cf2fa-default.json"),
3154        ),
3155        (
3156            "dbt",
3157            include_str!("../tests/fixtures/dbt/detconfig-806cf2fa-dbt.json"),
3158        ),

3161            include_str!("../tests/fixtures/dbt/detconfig-806cf2fa-rich_dbt.json"),
3162        ),
3163    ];
3164
3165    /// `Config::default()` with its three environment-backed fields
3166    /// (`HERMIT_EPOCH`, `HERMIT_PRNG`, `HERMIT_SCHED_SEED`) pinned, so the
3167    /// result does not depend on the test process's environment.
3168    fn env_free(epoch: &str, seed: &str) -> DetConfig {
3169        let mut config = DetConfig::parse_from(["hermit", epoch, seed, "--sched-seed=0"]);
3170        config.sched_seed = None;
3171        config
3172    }
3173
3174    fn default_config() -> DetConfig {
3175        env_free("--epoch=2026-01-01T00:00:00Z", "--seed=0")
3176    }
3177
3178    /// Non-default values in fields that serialize as strings, floats,
3179    /// options, and nested arrays. The path holds legacy anchor key names, so
3180    /// a substring-based insertion would put a key inside a string value.
3181    fn rich_config() -> DetConfig {
3182        let mut config = env_free("--epoch=2000-12-31T23:59:59Z", "--seed=7");
3183        config.rng_seed = Some(13);
3184        config.sched_seed = Some(11);
3185        config.clock_multiplier = Some(1.5);
3186        config.chaos = true;
3187        config.record_preemptions_to = Some(PathBuf::from(
3188            "/tmp/a,\"detect_host_clock_futex_timeouts\":true,\"backend_tracks_process_children\":{",
3189        ));
3190        config.mountinfo_root_rewrites = vec![MountInfoRootRewrite {
3191            raw_mount_id: 41,
3192            deterministic_root: b"/".to_vec(),
3193            raw_root_prefix: Some(b"/tmp/x".to_vec()),
3194            deterministic_root_prefix: Some(b"/tmp".to_vec()),
3195            raw_mountpoint_prefix: None,
3196            deterministic_mountpoint_prefix: None,
3197        }];
3198        config.stacktrace_event = vec![(3, Some(PathBuf::from("/s")))];
3199        config
3200    }
3201
3202    fn cases() -> [(&'static str, DetConfig); 3] {
3203        [
3204            ("default", default_config()),
3205            (
3206                "dbt",
3207                prepare_backend_config(default_config(), Backend::Dbt),
3208            ),
3209            (
3210                "rich_dbt",
3211                prepare_backend_config(rich_config(), Backend::Dbt),
3212            ),
3213        ]
3214    }

3288            "unexpected error: {error}"

3292        let error = insert_legacy_dbt_detconfig_keys(present).unwrap_err();

3295            "unexpected error: {error}"
