5098    #[test]
5099    fn in_guest_trap_is_in_guest_liteinst_and_forces_site_patching_off() {
5100        assert!(Backend::Liteinst.is_in_guest_liteinst());
5101        assert!(Backend::InGuestTrap.is_in_guest_liteinst());
5102        for backend in [
5103            Backend::Ptrace,
5104            Backend::Dbt,
5105            Backend::Sabre,
5106            Backend::Kvm,
5107            Backend::E9patch,
5108        ] {
5109            assert!(!backend.is_in_guest_liteinst(), "{backend:?}");
5110            assert_eq!(backend.runtime_name(), backend.as_str(), "{backend:?}");
5111            assert_eq!(forced_site_patching_value(backend), None, "{backend:?}");
5112        }
5113        assert_eq!(Backend::InGuestTrap.as_str(), "in-guest-trap");
5114        assert_eq!(Backend::InGuestTrap.runtime_name(), "liteinst");
5115        assert_eq!(Backend::Liteinst.runtime_name(), "liteinst");
5116        // liteinst leaves the variable to the guest environment.
5117        assert_eq!(forced_site_patching_value(Backend::Liteinst), None);
5118        assert_eq!(forced_site_patching_value(Backend::InGuestTrap), Some("0"));
5119        // The CLI spelling parses to the variant and back.
5120        assert_eq!(
5121            <Backend as ValueEnum>::from_str("in-guest-trap", false),
5122            Ok(Backend::InGuestTrap)
5123        );
5124        assert!(Backend::ALL.contains(&Backend::InGuestTrap));
5125        for backend in Backend::ALL {
5126            let name = backend
5127                .to_possible_value()
5128                .expect("every backend is a visible CLI value");
5129            assert_eq!(name.get_name(), backend.as_str(), "{backend:?}");
5130        }
5131    }
5132
5133    /// in-guest-trap sets `REVERIE_LITEINST_SITE_PATCHING=0` on the guest
5134    /// command when the guest environment leaves it unset or already `0`, and
5135    /// refuses, before anything starts, an environment that sets any other
5136    /// value; the refusal names the value and both ways out. liteinst neither
5137    /// refuses nor changes the variable.
5138    #[test]
5139    #[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
5140    fn in_guest_trap_refuses_a_guest_environment_that_turns_site_patching_on() {
5141        let name = reverie_liteinst::SITE_PATCHING_ENV;
5142        let mut unset = Command::new("/bin/true");
5143        unset.env_remove(name);
5144        assert_eq!(
5145            in_guest_trap_site_patching_conflict(&unset, Backend::InGuestTrap),
5146            None
5147        );
5148        force_in_guest_site_patching(&mut unset, Backend::InGuestTrap);
5149        assert_eq!(unset.get_env(name).as_deref(), Some(OsStr::new("0")));
5150
5151        let mut zero = Command::new("/bin/true");
5152        zero.env(name, "0");
5153        assert_eq!(
5154            in_guest_trap_site_patching_conflict(&zero, Backend::InGuestTrap),
5155            None
5156        );
5157
5158        for value in ["1", "", "yes", " 0"] {
5159            let mut command = Command::new("/bin/true");
5160            command.env(name, value);
5161            let conflict = in_guest_trap_site_patching_conflict(&command, Backend::InGuestTrap)
5162                .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{value:?} was accepted"));
5163            assert!(conflict.contains(&format!("{name}={value},")), "{conflict}");
5164            assert!(conflict.contains("--backend=liteinst"), "{conflict}");
5165            assert!(conflict.contains("drop the variable"), "{conflict}");
5166            // The whole refusal path stops on it first, before the program,
5167            // kernel or timeslice checks, and leaves the value untouched.
5168            let error = refuse_in_guest_liteinst_run(
5169                &mut command,
5170                &DetConfig::default(),
5171                Backend::InGuestTrap,
5172            )
5173            .unwrap_err();
5174            let refusal = error
5175                .downcast_ref::<LiteinstInGuestRefusal>()
5176                .expect("a typed in-guest refusal, which the CLI reports as exit 122");
5177            assert!(
5178                refusal.to_string().starts_with(
5179                    "--backend=in-guest-trap (in-guest LiteInst, site patching off) refuses \
5180                     this run: the guest environment sets"
5181                ),
5182                "{refusal}"
5183            );
5184            assert_eq!(command.get_env(name).as_deref(), Some(OsStr::new(value)));
5185
5186            // liteinst takes the variable from the guest environment as it is.
5187            let mut liteinst = Command::new("/bin/true");
5188            liteinst.env(name, value);
5189            assert_eq!(
5190                in_guest_trap_site_patching_conflict(&liteinst, Backend::Liteinst),
5191                None
5192            );
5193            force_in_guest_site_patching(&mut liteinst, Backend::Liteinst);
5194            assert_eq!(liteinst.get_env(name).as_deref(), Some(OsStr::new(value)));
5195        }
5196    }
5197
5198    /// The coordinator's postcondition for an in-guest-trap run: a record
5199    /// with no patched and no candidate site passes; a patched site, a
5200    /// candidate, an unmeasured count or a missing record is refused, with a
5201    /// typed in-guest refusal and a determinism loss. Other backends are not
5202    /// held to it.
5203    #[test]
5204    #[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
5205    fn an_in_guest_trap_run_that_patched_a_site_is_refused_after_it_ends() {
5206        let record = |candidates: Option<u64>, patched: Option<u64>| {
5207            reverie::DispatchStats::new(
5208                "in-guest-trap",
5209                reverie::DispatchCounters::ZERO,
5210                reverie::SiteCounters {
5211                    candidates,
5212                    patched,
5213                    fell_back: Some(0),
5214                },
5215            )
5216        };
5217        let clean = record(Some(0), Some(0));
5218        assert_eq!(
5219            in_guest_trap_patching_evidence(Backend::InGuestTrap, Some(&clean), 1),
5220            None
5221        );
5222        for (candidates, patched, shown) in [
5223            (Some(3), Some(3), "3 patched and 3 candidate"),
5224            (Some(1), Some(0), "0 patched and 1 candidate"),
5225            (Some(0), None, "unmeasured patched and 0 candidate"),
5226        ] {
5227            let reason = in_guest_trap_patching_evidence(
5228                Backend::InGuestTrap,
5229                Some(&record(candidates, patched)),
5230                1,
5231            )
5232            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{candidates:?} {patched:?} was accepted"));
5233            assert!(reason.contains(shown), "{reason}");
5234        }
5235        assert!(
5236            in_guest_trap_patching_evidence(Backend::InGuestTrap, None, 1)
5237                .is_some_and(|reason| reason.contains("no LiteInst dispatch record"))
5238        );
5239        // An empty record from guests that collected nothing is not zero.
5240        assert!(
5241            in_guest_trap_patching_evidence(Backend::InGuestTrap, Some(&clean), 0)
5242                .is_some_and(|reason| reason.contains("no guest process reported"))
5243        );
5244        let patched = record(Some(3), Some(3));
5245        assert_eq!(
5246            in_guest_trap_patching_evidence(Backend::Liteinst, Some(&patched), 1),
5247            None
5248        );
5249        assert_eq!(
5250            in_guest_trap_patching_evidence(Backend::Liteinst, None, 0),
5251            None
5252        );
5253
5254        refuse_in_guest_trap_patching(Backend::InGuestTrap, None).unwrap();
5255        let error = refuse_in_guest_trap_patching(
5256            Backend::InGuestTrap,
5257            in_guest_trap_patching_evidence(Backend::InGuestTrap, Some(&patched), 1),
5258        )
5259        .unwrap_err();
5260        assert!(
5261            error.downcast_ref::<LiteinstInGuestRefusal>().is_some(),
5262            "{error:#}"
5263        );
5264        assert!(
5265            error
5266                .to_string()
5267                .starts_with("--backend=in-guest-trap (in-guest LiteInst, site patching off)"),
5268            "{error:#}"
5269        );
5270        assert!(
5271            detcore::detlog::determinism_loss().is_some_and(|loss| loss.contains("3 patched")),
5272            "the refusal must record a determinism loss"
5273        );
5274    }

5292    #[test]
5293    #[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
5294    fn in_guest_liteinst_refuses_a_kernel_older_than_6_5() {
5295        for release in [
5296            "6.5.0",
5297            "6.5",
5298            "6.10.3-200.fc40.x86_64",
5299            "7.1.3-0_fbk0_rc18",
5300            "10.0",
5301        ] {

5303        }

5309        }

5313                "{release:?}"
5314            );
5315        }
5316        // No assertion about the kernel running the tests: that would make
5317        // the test's result depend on the host, and the releases above
5318        // already test both sides of the floor.
5319    }

5321    #[test]
5322    #[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
5323    fn in_guest_liteinst_refuses_secure_execution_images() {
5324        use interp::startup_fixtures::dynamic_executable;
5325
5326        let directory = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
5327        let bytes = dynamic_executable(false);
5328        let gap = |name: &str, mode: u32| {
5329            let program = write_guest_program(directory.path(), name, &bytes, mode);
5330            in_guest_liteinst_program_gap(&mut Command::new(&program)).unwrap()
5331        };
5332        for (name, mode) in [("setuid", 0o4755), ("setgid", 0o2755)] {
5333            let refusal = gap(name, mode).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{name} was not refused"));
5334            assert!(refusal.contains("secure-execution mode"), "{refusal}");
5335        }
5336        // Without group execute the kernel treats set-group-ID as mandatory
5337        // locking, not a credential change, so the loader honours LD_PRELOAD.
5338        assert_eq!(gap("setgid-no-group-exec", 0o2744), None);
5339    }
5340
5341    #[test]
5342    #[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
5343    fn in_guest_liteinst_checks_scripts_through_their_interpreter() {
5344        use interp::startup_fixtures::*;
5345
5346        let directory = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
5347        let dynamic = write_guest_program(
5348            directory.path(),
5349            "dynamic",
5350            &dynamic_executable(false),
5351            0o755,
5352        );
5353        let static_elf = write_guest_program(
5354            directory.path(),
5355            "static",
5356            &elf(EM_X86_64, &[(PT_LOAD, b"code")]),
5357            0o755,
5358        );
5359
5360        let script = write_guest_program(
5361            directory.path(),
5362            "to-static",
5363            &shebang_to(&static_elf),
5364            0o755,
5365        );
5366        let gap = in_guest_liteinst_program_gap(&mut Command::new(&script))
5367            .unwrap()
5368            .expect("a script run by a static interpreter was not refused");
5369        assert!(
5370            gap.contains(&static_elf.display().to_string()) && gap.contains("statically linked"),
5371            "{gap}"
5372        );
5373
5374        // A relative interpreter resolves against the guest's working directory.
5375        let relative = write_guest_program(directory.path(), "relative", b"#!static\n", 0o755);
5376        let gap =
5377            in_guest_liteinst_program_gap(Command::new(&relative).current_dir(directory.path()))
5378                .unwrap()
5379                .expect("a relative static interpreter was not refused");
5380        assert!(gap.contains("statically linked"), "{gap}");
5381
5382        // The kernel keeps a carriage return in the interpreter path, so a
5383        // script with CRLF line endings runs `interp\r`, not `interp`.
5384        write_guest_program(
5385            directory.path(),
5386            "interp",
5387            &dynamic_executable(false),
5388            0o755,
5389        );
5390        let interp_cr = write_guest_program(
5391            directory.path(),
5392            "interp\r",
5393            &elf(EM_X86_64, &[(PT_LOAD, b"code")]),
5394            0o755,
5395        );
5396        let mut crlf = b"#!".to_vec();
5397        crlf.extend_from_slice(directory.path().join("interp").as_os_str().as_bytes());
5398        crlf.extend_from_slice(b"\r\necho script\r\n");
5399        let crlf = write_guest_program(directory.path(), "crlf", &crlf, 0o755);
5400        let gap = in_guest_liteinst_program_gap(&mut Command::new(&crlf))
5401            .unwrap()
5402            .expect("a CRLF script run by a static interp\\r was not refused");
5403        assert!(
5404            gap.contains(&interp_cr.display().to_string()) && gap.contains("statically linked"),
5405            "{gap}"
5406        );
5407
5408        // A #! line the kernel's script handler declines runs no interpreter:
5409        // the kernel fails the execve or tries another handler, so it is
5410        // refused.
5411        let mut truncated = b"#!/".to_vec();
5412        truncated.resize(256, b'a');
5413        for (name, bytes) in [
5414            ("empty", b"#! \t\nbody\n".to_vec()),
5415            ("truncated", truncated),
5416        ] {
5417            let program = write_guest_program(directory.path(), name, &bytes, 0o755);
5418            let gap = in_guest_liteinst_program_gap(&mut Command::new(&program))
5419                .unwrap()
5420                .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{name} was not refused"));
5421            assert!(
5422                gap.contains("the kernel's script handler declines it"),
5423                "{name}: {gap}"
5424            );
5425        }
5426
5427        // Four nested scripts are followed to the ELF image; a fifth is refused.
5428        let mut target = dynamic;
5429        for level in 1..=5 {
5430            target = write_guest_program(
5431                directory.path(),
5432                &format!("level-{level}"),
5433                &shebang_to(&target),
5434                0o755,
5435            );
5436            let gap = in_guest_liteinst_program_gap(&mut Command::new(&target)).unwrap();
5437            if level <= MAX_INTERPRETER_DEPTH {
5438                assert_eq!(gap, None, "level {level}");
5439            } else {
5440                let gap = gap.expect("five nested scripts were not refused");
5441                assert!(gap.contains("nest more than 4 deep"), "{gap}");
5442            }
5443        }
5444    }
5445
5446    #[test]
5447    #[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
5448    fn in_guest_liteinst_refuses_a_fifo_without_blocking() {
5449        let directory = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
5450        let fifo = directory.path().join("fifo");
5451        let fifo_c = std::ffi::CString::new(fifo.as_os_str().as_bytes()).unwrap();
5452        assert_eq!(unsafe { libc::mkfifo(fifo_c.as_ptr(), 0o700) }, 0);
5453        let script = write_guest_program(directory.path(), "to-fifo", &shebang_to(&fifo), 0o755);
5454        // A socket cannot be opened at all (ENXIO), and opening a device can
5455        // act on it; both must be refused, not reported as unreadable.
5456        let socket = directory.path().join("socket");
5457        let _listener = std::os::unix::net::UnixListener::bind(&socket).unwrap();
5458        let to_socket =
5459            write_guest_program(directory.path(), "to-socket", &shebang_to(&socket), 0o755);
5460        let device = PathBuf::from("/dev/null");
5461        let to_device =
5462            write_guest_program(directory.path(), "to-device", &shebang_to(&device), 0o755);
5463        // `./fifo` reaches the check through find_program's working-directory
5464        // branch, which does not require a regular file; the scripts reach the
5465        // FIFO, the socket and the device as their interpreters. Nothing opens
5466        // the FIFO for writing, so a blocking open or read would never return:
5467        // each check runs on its own thread and must answer within 10 s.
5468        let cases = [
5469            (
5470                "program",
5471                PathBuf::from("./fifo"),
5472                Some(directory.path().to_owned()),
5473                fs::canonicalize(&fifo).unwrap(),
5474            ),
5475            ("interpreter", script, None, fifo.clone()),
5476            ("socket interpreter", to_socket, None, socket.clone()),
5477            ("device interpreter", to_device, None, device),
5478        ];
5479        for (name, program, current_dir, shown) in cases {
5480            let (sender, receiver) = std::sync::mpsc::channel();
5481            std::thread::spawn(move || {
5482                let mut command = Command::new(&program);
5483                if let Some(directory) = current_dir {
5484                    command.current_dir(directory);
5485                }
5486                let gap = in_guest_liteinst_program_gap(&mut command)
5487                    .map_err(|error| format!("{error:#}"));
5488                let _ = sender.send(gap);
5489            });
5490            let gap = receiver
5491                .recv_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(10))
5492                .unwrap_or_else(|_| panic!("{name}: the check blocked on the FIFO"))
5493                .unwrap()
5494                .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{name}: the FIFO was not refused"));
5495            assert!(
5496                gap.contains(&format!("{} is not a regular file", shown.display())),
5497                "{name}: {gap}"
5498            );
5499        }
5500    }
5501
5502    #[test]
5503    #[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
5504    fn in_guest_liteinst_pins_the_program_the_launch_resolves() {
5505        use interp::startup_fixtures::*;
5506
5507        let directory = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
5508        // A name that no host PATH directory holds.
5509        let name = "hermit-3520-static";
5510        let static_elf = write_guest_program(
5511            directory.path(),
5512            name,
5513            &elf(EM_X86_64, &[(PT_LOAD, b"code")]),
5514            0o755,
5515        );
5516        let search = format!("/nonexistent-directory:{}", directory.path().display());
5517        // The launch resolves the program with `find_program` on the command it
5518        // is given. Builds the command twice: once for the launch's own
5519        // resolution, once for the check, which must agree with it.
5520        let check = |build: &dyn Fn() -> Command| {
5521            let launch = build().find_program();
5522            let mut command = build();
5523            let arg0 = command.get_arg0().to_owned();
5524            let gap = in_guest_liteinst_program_gap(&mut command);
5525            match launch {
5526                Ok(program) => {
5527                    let gap = gap.unwrap().expect("the static image was not refused");
5528                    assert!(gap.contains("is statically linked"), "{gap}");
5529                    assert_eq!(Path::new(command.get_program()), program);
5530                    assert_eq!(command.get_arg0(), arg0);
5531                    // Pinned: the launch's own resolution returns it unchanged.
5532                    assert_eq!(command.find_program().unwrap(), program);
5533                    assert_eq!(
5534                        fs::canonicalize(&program).unwrap(),
5535                        fs::canonicalize(&static_elf).unwrap()
5536                    );
5537                    true
5538                }
5539                Err(_) => {
5540                    let error = gap.expect_err("the check found what the launch cannot");
5541                    assert!(
5542                        format!("{error:#}").contains("cannot find guest program"),
5543                        "{error:#}"
5544                    );
5545                    false
5546                }
5547            }
5548        };
5549
5550        // A path with a slash resolves against the guest's working directory.
5551        assert!(check(&|| {
5552            let mut command = Command::new(format!("./{name}"));
5553            command.current_dir(directory.path());
5554            command
5555        }));
5556        // A bare name is searched for in the command's PATH once the base
5557        // environment is cleared, skipping entries without such a file.
5558        assert!(check(&|| {
5559            let mut command = Command::new(name);
5560            command.env_clear().env("PATH", &search);
5561            command
5562        }));
5563        // Without a cleared environment the launch searches Hermit's own PATH,
5564        // not the command's, so the static image is not found by either.
5565        assert!(!check(&|| {
5566            let mut command = Command::new(name);
5567            command.env("PATH", &search);
5568            command
5569        }));
5570        // A cleared environment without PATH (`--base-env=empty`) searches
5571        // Hermit's working directory, not the guest's and not /bin:/usr/bin.
5572        assert!(!check(&|| {
5573            let mut command = Command::new(name);
5574            command.env_clear().current_dir(directory.path());
5575            command
5576        }));
5577        // Not executable, or missing: the launch cannot run it, and neither
5578        // resolution finds it.
5579        fs::set_permissions(&static_elf, fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o644)).unwrap();
5580        assert!(!check(&|| {
5581            let mut command = Command::new(name);
5582            command.env_clear().env("PATH", &search);
5583            command
5584        }));
5585        assert!(!check(&|| Command::new(&static_elf)));
5586        assert!(!check(&|| Command::new("hermit-no-such-program-3520")));
5587    }

5633    /// ⚠️ THE REGRESSION agent(hermit-007)'s CODEX LANE CAUGHT, PINNED FOR THE
5634    /// SIGNAL BAND. This test fails if the signal half stops being covered; it
5635    /// does NOT prove the predicate can never drift again, because the
5636    /// `122..=127` half is still a hard-coded literal that a future reserved
5637    /// value would fall outside exactly as 130 did.
5638    /// `liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown` hard-coded
5639    /// `Exited(122..=127)`. When the reserved set grew to include the signal
5640    /// band, `Exited(130)` fell outside it and is not `Signaled(_, _)` -- it is a
5641    /// status hermit CHOSE -- so LiteInst skipped the forced shutdown and
5642    /// `clean_up` could wait forever on `handle.await`. A reachable hang,
5643    /// introduced by widening the reserved set in one place and not the other.
5644    ///
5645    /// The 130 and 143 rows are the ones with teeth: revert the predicate to the
5646    /// bare range and they fail.
5647    #[test]
5648    #[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
5649    fn liteinst_forced_shutdown_covers_every_status_hermit_reserves() {
5650        // The signal band. Not `Signaled`: hermit CHOSE these codes, because a
5651        // namespace init cannot produce a genuine signalled wait status.
5652        assert!(
5653            liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(130)),
5654            "128+SIGINT"
5655        );
5656        assert!(
5657            liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(143)),
5658            "128+SIGTERM"
5659        );
5660        assert!(
5661            liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(129)),
5662            "128+SIGHUP"
5663        );
5664
5665        // The pre-existing reserved band, unchanged.
5666        assert!(liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(122)));
5667        assert!(liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(125)));
5668        assert!(liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(127)));
5669
5670        // A real signal death still forces shutdown.
5671        assert!(liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Signaled(
5672            nix::sys::signal::Signal::SIGKILL,
5673            false
5674        )));
5675
5676        // ⚠️ CONTROLS, so a predicate that simply returned `true` would fail
5677        // this test rather than pass it. An ordinary guest exit must NOT force a
5678        // shutdown; 128 is excluded because there is no signal 0, and 200 is
5679        // above SIGRTMAX so it is a guest's own number.
5680        assert!(!liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(0)));
5681        assert!(!liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(1)));
5682        assert!(!liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(121)));
5683        assert!(!liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(128)));
5684        assert!(!liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(200)));
5685        // ⚠️ THE BAND EDGES, WHICH THIS TEST STOPPED SHORT OF. It went up to 143
5686        // and then jumped to 200, so `MAX_SIGNO` could shrink from 64 to 15 with
5687        // every row still green -- proved vacuous by agent(hermit-005)'s codex
5688        // lane. 192 is `128 + SIGRTMAX`, the last status in the band; 193 is the
5689        // first outside it. Testing a range in the middle pins nothing at either end.
5690        assert!(
5691            liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(192)),
5692            "128 + SIGRTMAX is still a signal death and must force shutdown"
5693        );
5694        assert!(
5695            !liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(193)),
5696            "above SIGRTMAX the status is the guest's own and must NOT force shutdown"
5697        );
5698    }

5700    /// ⚠️ The bare `NotFound` here was read as a git-worktree bug and cost real
5701    /// investigation time. The message must name the mount namespace, because
5702    /// the directory it is complaining about usually exists on the host.
5703    #[test]
5704    fn a_cwd_under_tmp_is_explained_by_the_container_tmpfs_not_by_a_missing_directory() {
5705        let error = std::io::Error::from(std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound);
5706        let rendered = format!(
5707            "{}",
5708            super::kvm_cwd_resolution_error(std::path::Path::new("/tmp/agent-checkout"), &error)
5709        );
5710        assert!(
5711            rendered.contains("private, empty tmpfs"),
5712            "must name the mechanism: {rendered}"
5713        );
5714        assert!(
5715            rendered.contains("--workdir"),
5716            "must name a way out: {rendered}"
5717        );
5718        assert!(
5719            rendered.contains("/tmp/agent-checkout"),
5720            "must still name the path asked for: {rendered}"
5721        );
5722    }
5723
5724    /// The explanation must NOT be attached to failures it does not explain --
5725    /// an error that volunteers the wrong cause is what produced the original
5726    /// misdiagnosis, in the other direction.
5727    #[test]
5728    fn the_tmpfs_explanation_is_withheld_where_it_does_not_apply() {
5729        let not_found = std::io::Error::from(std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound);
5730        // ⚠️ A CONSTANT ABSOLUTE PATH, NOT ONE READ FROM THE ENVIRONMENT. The only
5731        // property this arm needs is "outside /tmp", and a literal that is outside
5732        // /tmp by construction supplies it without asking the runner anything.
5733        //
5734        // The earlier form wrote a developer-specific home path, which made
5735        // `scripts/check-portable-paths.sh` red on main -- correctly: that gate
5736        // rejects such a path anywhere in a tracked build or run file, tests and
5737        // COMMENTS included, so spelling one out even to explain this would trip
5738        // it. Replacing it by reading $HOME then made the test depend on the
5739        // runner instead: measured by `agent(codex-rev-2641)`, HOME unset, empty,
5740        // relative and /tmp-rooted gave three different fixtures and one failure.
5741        // A unit test that consults the environment is answering a question about
5742        // the environment.
5743        //
5744        // ⚠️ AND NO GUARD IS NEEDED HERE, WHICH IS THE POINT OF USING A CONSTANT.
5745        // The $HOME form carried `assert!(!outside_path.starts_with("/tmp"))` to
5746        // stop a /tmp-rooted HOME making this vacuous. That assertion could never
5747        // fire: the `!outside.contains("tmpfs")` check below sits ABOVE it in
5748        // source order and panics first in exactly that case. It was decorative,
5749        // and an assertion over a compile-time constant would be too, so there is
5750        // none. The fixture cannot drift into /tmp because nothing computes it.
5751        let outside_path = std::path::Path::new("/nonexistent-home/wt");
5752        let outside = format!(
5753            "{}",
5754            super::kvm_cwd_resolution_error(outside_path, &not_found)
5755        );
5756        assert!(!outside.contains("tmpfs"), "not a /tmp path: {outside}");
5757
5758        // `/tmp` itself resolves in the guest -- the mount POINT exists -- so a
5759        // failure there is something else and must not be mislabelled.
5760        let tmp_itself = format!(
5761            "{}",
5762            super::kvm_cwd_resolution_error(std::path::Path::new("/tmp"), &not_found)
5763        );
5764        assert!(!tmp_itself.contains("tmpfs"), "/tmp itself: {tmp_itself}");
5765
5766        // A different errno under /tmp is a different fault (permissions, say).
5767        let denied = std::io::Error::from(std::io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied);
5768        let other = format!(
5769            "{}",
5770            super::kvm_cwd_resolution_error(std::path::Path::new("/tmp/x"), &denied)
5771        );
5772        assert!(!other.contains("tmpfs"), "not NotFound: {other}");
5773    }

5775    /// A build-flag message must not assert a machine condition it never tested.
5776    ///
5777    /// ⚠️ THIS IS THE FOURTH DIAGNOSTIC TONIGHT THAT NAMED AN UNESTABLISHED CAUSE,
5778    /// after the sigpipe checker, the manifest gate and the LiteInst pidfd error.
5779    /// "DBT support was not included in this build" reads as "this machine cannot
5780    /// run DBT", and cost four or five unnecessary abstentions across the fleet --
5781    /// agents declined to make DBT claims on a box where DynamoRIO is installed
5782    /// and `--features dbt` builds and runs. The absence is a build flag, and the
5783    /// message must say which flag and admit what it did not check.
5784    #[test]
5785    fn an_unbuilt_backend_names_the_flag_and_claims_nothing_about_the_machine() {
5786        let feature_disabled_backends: &[(Backend, &str)] = &[
5787            #[cfg(not(feature = "dbt"))]
5788            (Backend::Dbt, "dbt"),
5789            #[cfg(not(feature = "sabre"))]
5790            (Backend::Sabre, "sabre"),
5791            #[cfg(not(feature = "e9patch"))]
5792            (Backend::E9patch, "e9patch"),
5793            #[cfg(not(feature = "liteinst"))]
5794            (Backend::Liteinst, "liteinst"),
5795            // in-guest-trap is the same runtime behind the same feature.
5796            #[cfg(not(feature = "liteinst"))]
5797            (Backend::InGuestTrap, "liteinst"),
5798        ];
5799
5800        for &(backend, feature) in feature_disabled_backends {
5801            let reason = backend
5802                .unavailable_reason()
5803                .expect("a feature-disabled backend must report why it is unavailable");
5804            assert!(
5805                reason.contains(feature),
5806                "{backend:?}: a build-flag message must name the flag to rebuild with, got: {reason}"
5807            );
5808            assert!(
5809                reason.contains("has not been checked"),
5810                "{backend:?}: a build-flag message must not imply the machine was tested, got: {reason}"
5811            );
5812        }
5813    }

5935    #[test]
5936    fn recording_inventory_preserves_missing_and_non_recording_cases() {
5937        let parent = tempfile::tempdir().expect("failed to create recording inventory parent");
5938        let missing = parent.path().join("missing");
5939        assert!(
5940            HermitData::with_dir(&missing)
5941                .try_recordings()
5942                .expect("a missing data directory should be empty")
5943                .is_empty()
5944        );
5945
5946        let inventory = parent.path().join("inventory");
5947        fs::create_dir(&inventory).expect("failed to create recording inventory");
5948        let recording: Id = "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef"
5949            .parse()
5950            .expect("valid recording ID");
5951        fs::create_dir(inventory.join(recording.to_string()))
5952            .expect("failed to create recording directory");
5953        fs::create_dir(inventory.join("not-a-recording"))
5954            .expect("failed to create invalid-name directory");
5955        fs::write(
5956            inventory.join("fedcba9876543210fedcba9876543210"),
5957            b"not a directory",
5958        )
5959        .expect("failed to create valid-looking non-directory");
5960
5961        assert_eq!(
5962            HermitData::with_dir(&inventory)
5963                .try_recordings()
5964                .expect("recording inventory should be readable"),
5965            vec![recording]
5966        );
5967        assert_eq!(
5968            HermitData::with_dir(&inventory)
5969                .recordings()
5970                .collect::<Vec<_>>(),
5971            vec![recording]
5972        );
5973    }

5994            .expect_err("a partial inventory must not be returned");

5998                .contains("Failed to read an entry in recording inventory"),

6026    /// Regression test for the `hermit run --verify` empty-stdin bug: a pipe
6027    /// (non-seekable) fed to hermit must be buffered and replayed *identically*
6028    /// to every run of `--verify`. Before the fix the output-capturing backend
6029    /// used `Stdio::null()`, so piped input was silently dropped and both runs
6030    /// saw empty input (a false "deterministic" pass, e.g. `gcc -x c -`
6031    /// compiling nothing). This asserts the reserved snapshot replays the exact
6032    /// bytes twice.
6033    #[test]
6034    fn output_stdin_snapshot_replays_pipe_to_repeated_runs() {
6035        use std::io::Read;
6036        use std::io::Write;
6037        use std::os::fd::FromRawFd;
6038
6039        // A pipe is non-seekable, exercising the buffer-into-tempfile path that
6040        // matches the real `echo prog | hermit run --verify` scenario.
6041        let mut fds = [0i32; 2];
6042        assert_eq!(unsafe { libc::pipe(fds.as_mut_ptr()) }, 0);
6043        // SAFETY: pipe(2) just returned two fresh owned descriptors.
6044        let read_end = unsafe { fs::File::from_raw_fd(fds[0]) };
6045        let mut write_end = unsafe { fs::File::from_raw_fd(fds[1]) };
6046
6047        let payload = b"int main(){return 0;}\n";
6048        write_end.write_all(payload).unwrap();
6049        // Close the write end so draining the pipe hits EOF.
6050        drop(write_end);
6051
6052        reserve_output_stdin_snapshot(Some(read_end)).unwrap();
6053
6054        // Two successive runs (as `--verify` performs) must each read the full
6055        // payload from the start.
6056        for run in 0..2 {
6057            let mut file = output_backend_stdin_file()
6058                .unwrap()
6059                .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("run {run}: expected a replayable stdin snapshot"));
6060            let mut got = Vec::new();
6061            file.read_to_end(&mut got).unwrap();
6062            assert_eq!(got, payload, "run {run} stdin replay mismatch");
6063        }
6064
6065        let mut file = output_backend_stdin_file().unwrap().unwrap();
6066        let error = file
6067            .write_all(b"guest-must-not-change-the-snapshot")
6068            .expect_err("the replayed stdin must be read-only");
6069        assert_eq!(error.raw_os_error(), Some(libc::EBADF));
6070    }

6073    #[cfg(feature = "liteinst")]
6074    fn liteinst_reserved_failures_require_scheduler_cancellation() {
6075        for status in 122..=127 {
6076            assert!(liteinst_requires_forced_shutdown(ExitStatus::Exited(
6077                status

6084    /// A SaBRe guest that never reaches the coordinator ran with no Detcore in
6085    /// the loop at all. The diagnosis must say so in those terms -- a
6086    /// zero-syscall SaBRe run is not a weak result, it is *no* result -- and it
6087    /// must name the statically linked ELF case, which is the shape that
6088    /// reaches this path in practice because a static client has no dynamic
6089    /// loader through which SaBRe could regain control.
6090    #[test]
6091    #[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
6092    fn uninstrumented_sabre_guest_is_reported_as_no_determinization() {
6093        let message = sabre_uninstrumented_guest_message(&ExitStatus::Exited(0));
6094        assert!(
6095            message.contains("no determinization at all"),
6096            "must not present an uninstrumented run as a weaker guarantee: {message}"
6097        );
6098        assert!(
6099            message.contains("Detcore coordinator"),
6100            "must name the authority that was never reached: {message}"
6101        );
6102        assert!(
6103            message.contains("statically linked ELF"),
6104            "must name the dominant cause so the reader can act: {message}"
6105        );
6106        // The exit status is carried because a successful-looking status is
6107        // exactly what makes this failure mode dangerous.
6108        assert!(
6109            message.contains("Exited(0)"),
6110            "must carry the observed status: {message}"
6111        );
6112        assert!(
6113            sabre_uninstrumented_guest_message(&ExitStatus::Exited(139)).contains("Exited(139)"),
6114            "must carry a failing status too"
6115        );
6116    }

6118    /// The old two-way banner treated both `guest_rpc_observed=false` and an
6119    /// engaged zero-fallback run as `sabre-exercised`. Assert the three states
6120    /// together so no pair can collapse back onto one value.
6121    #[test]
6122    #[cfg(feature = "sabre")]
6123    fn sabre_reach_states_are_pairwise_distinct() {
6124        let no_detcore = sabre_reach_state(false, 0);
6125        let degraded = sabre_reach_state(true, 1);
6126        let exercised = sabre_reach_state(true, 0);
6127
6128        assert_eq!(no_detcore, "no-detcore-reached");
6129        assert_eq!(degraded, "degraded-ptrace-fallback");
6130        assert_eq!(exercised, "sabre-exercised");
6131        assert_ne!(no_detcore, degraded);
6132        assert_ne!(no_detcore, exercised);
6133        assert_ne!(degraded, exercised);
6134        assert_eq!(
6135            sabre_reach_state(false, 9),
6136            "no-detcore-reached",
6137            "absence of a guest RPC must dominate any fallback count"
6138        );
6139    }

6274    #[test]
6275    fn kvm_backend_config_marks_concurrent_process_children() {
6276        let defaults = super::DetConfig::default();
6277        assert!(!defaults.sequentialize_threads);
6278        let nonsequential = prepare_backend_config(defaults, Backend::Kvm);
6279        assert!(nonsequential.backend.provides_process_signal_control);
6280        assert!(nonsequential.backend.emulates_child_waits);
6281        assert!(!may_observe_signal_dequeues(&nonsequential));
6282        assert!(!requires_signal_control(&nonsequential));
6283
6284        let config = super::DetConfig {
6285            sequentialize_threads: true,
6286            ..super::DetConfig::default()
6287        };
6288        let kvm = prepare_backend_config(config, Backend::Kvm);
6289        assert!(may_observe_signal_dequeues(&kvm));
6290        assert!(requires_signal_control(&kvm));
6291        assert!(kvm.backend.needs_killed_thread_rpc_cancellation);
6292        assert!(kvm.backend.tracks_process_children);
6293        assert!(!kvm.backend.runs_exit_robust_list);
6294        assert!(!kvm.backend.requires_thread_directed_process_signals);
6295        assert!(kvm.backend.virtualizes_capability_prctls);
6296        assert!(kvm.backend.defers_vfork_child_registration);
6297
6298        // A controlled run needs both the backend's process-signal control and
6299        // sequentialized threads; neither alone admits one.
6300        for backend in [
6301            Backend::Ptrace,
6302            Backend::Dbt,
6303            Backend::Sabre,
6304            Backend::Liteinst,
6305            Backend::InGuestTrap,
6306            Backend::E9patch,
6307        ] {
6308            let config = super::DetConfig {
6309                sequentialize_threads: true,
6310                ..super::DetConfig::default()
6311            };
6312            let prepared = prepare_backend_config(config, backend);
6313            assert!(!may_observe_signal_dequeues(&prepared), "{backend:?}");
6314            assert!(!requires_signal_control(&prepared), "{backend:?}");
6315        }

6318    /// Whether Detcore asks the backend to install process signal control for
6319    /// `config`: the bound Reverie checks before it offers the control.
6320    fn may_observe_signal_dequeues(config: &DetConfig) -> bool {
6321        <super::Detcore as reverie::Tool>::may_observe_signal_dequeues(config)
6322    }
6323
6324    /// Whether Reverie must refuse `config` on a runner that offers no
6325    /// process signal control, as Detcore's installation did before.
6326    fn requires_signal_control(config: &DetConfig) -> bool {
6327        <super::Detcore as reverie::Tool>::requires_signal_control(config)
6328    }
6329
6330    /// The values the hand-written assignments in `prepare_backend_config` and
6331    /// `run_kvm` gave each backend before Detcore read
6332    /// [`reverie::BackendCapabilities`], transcribed field by field. A backend
6333    /// identity check became the capability it selected:
6334    /// `discover_live_file_metadata` split into the first four fields, the old
6335    /// `backend_reports_physical_process_exits` became two, and
6336    /// `backend_is_kvm` became five, of which the last two remain here:
6337    /// `refuses_nonleader_exec_with_enosys` became the backend's typed exec
6338    /// refusal (Reverie d78f770f), `failed_gettimeofday_may_store_host_time`
6339    /// the guest's storable-memory report (Reverie 8bfb450e) and
6340    /// `user_address_limit` the guest's user address limit query (Reverie
6341    /// 6f75cc4c). Four fields are newer than that transcription:
6342    /// `process_exits_complete_asynchronously` (Reverie 056c8cac), true only
6343    /// for in-guest LiteInst, whose process exits complete some time after the
6344    /// tool grants them, `supports_madv_dontneed` (Reverie 8d8697aa), true for
6345    /// every backend, and `reports_child_exit_publication` (Reverie 9cb6f7b7),
6346    /// true for ptrace and e9patch, whose tracer reports when the kernel
6347    /// publishes a process's exit to its parent, and
6348    /// `virtualizes_guest_sigalrm` (Reverie 7142ff8c), true only for in-guest
6349    /// LiteInst, whose runtime keeps a guest SIGALRM handler virtual (signal
6350    /// phase 1). `in-guest-trap` is in-guest LiteInst with site patching off,
6351    /// which changes how a syscall reaches the Tool and nothing the Tool host
6352    /// can do, so it has exactly in-guest LiteInst's row.
6353    fn golden_backend_capabilities(backend: Backend) -> serde_json::Value {
6354        let sabre = backend == Backend::Sabre;
6355        let kvm = backend == Backend::Kvm;
6356        let dbt = backend == Backend::Dbt;
6357        // LiteInst runs only through its in-guest runtime.
6358        let in_guest_liteinst = matches!(backend, Backend::Liteinst | Backend::InGuestTrap);
6359        serde_json::json!({
6360            "tool_shares_guest_descriptor_table": sabre,
6361            "rediscovers_descriptors_after_exec": sabre,
6362            "internal_pipe_turns_are_host_timed": sabre,
6363            "loopback_pollers_yield_to_peers": sabre,
6364            "guest_clock_reads_bypass_backend": sabre,
6365            "virtualizes_syscall_clobbers": sabre,
6366            "needs_killed_thread_rpc_cancellation": in_guest_liteinst || sabre || dbt || kvm,
6367            "reports_physical_process_exits": sabre,
6368            "process_exits_complete_asynchronously": in_guest_liteinst,
6369            "virtualizes_guest_sigalrm": in_guest_liteinst,
6370            "signal_interrupts_external_syscalls": sabre,
6371            "tracks_process_children": !dbt,
6372            "runs_exit_robust_list": backend == Backend::Ptrace,
6373            "requires_thread_directed_process_signals": dbt,
6374            "supports_parked_write_signal_interruption":
6375                matches!(backend, Backend::Ptrace | Backend::E9patch),
6376            "defers_vfork_child_registration": kvm,
6377            "virtualizes_capability_prctls": kvm,
6378            // `run_kvm` set these two after `prepare_backend_config`.
6379            "virtualizes_cpuid": kvm,
6380            "supports_madvise": !kvm,
6381            "supports_madv_dontneed": true,
6382            "reports_child_exit_publication":
6383                matches!(backend, Backend::Ptrace | Backend::E9patch),
6384            "provides_process_signal_control": kvm,
6385            "emulates_child_waits": kvm,
6386        })
6387    }
6388

6390    fn backend_capabilities_match_the_golden_table() {
6391        for backend in [
6392            Backend::Ptrace,
6393            Backend::Kvm,
6394            Backend::E9patch,
6395            Backend::Liteinst,
6396            Backend::InGuestTrap,

6446    /// `Config` with its three environment-backed fields (`HERMIT_EPOCH`,
6447    /// `HERMIT_PRNG`, `HERMIT_SCHED_SEED`) pinned, so the encoding does not
6448    /// depend on the test process's environment.
6449    fn env_free_detconfig(epoch: &str, seed: &str) -> DetConfig {
6450        use clap::Parser;
6451        let mut config = DetConfig::parse_from(["hermit", epoch, seed, "--sched-seed=0"]);
6452        config.sched_seed = None;
6453        config
6454    }
6455
6456    /// Non-default values in fields that serialize as strings, floats,
6457    /// options and nested arrays. The path contains legacy key names, so an
6458    /// encoder that edited text would put a key inside a string value.
6459    fn rich_detconfig() -> DetConfig {
6460        let mut config = env_free_detconfig("--epoch=2000-12-31T23:59:59Z", "--seed=7");
6461        config.rng_seed = Some(13);
6462        config.sched_seed = Some(11);
6463        config.clock_multiplier = Some(1.5);
6464        config.chaos = true;
6465        config.record_preemptions_to = Some(std::path::PathBuf::from(
6466            "/tmp/a,\"detect_host_clock_futex_timeouts\":true,\"backend_tracks_process_children\":{",
6467        ));
6468        config.mountinfo_root_rewrites = vec![detcore_model::config::MountInfoRootRewrite {
6469            raw_mount_id: 41,
6470            deterministic_root: b"/".to_vec(),
6471            raw_root_prefix: Some(b"/tmp/x".to_vec()),
6472            deterministic_root_prefix: Some(b"/tmp".to_vec()),
6473            raw_mountpoint_prefix: None,
6474            deterministic_mountpoint_prefix: None,
6475        }];
6476        config.stacktrace_event = vec![(3, Some(std::path::PathBuf::from("/s")))];
6477        config
6478    }
6479
6480    /// `HERMIT_DBT_DETCONFIG` is in the guest's environment, so a guest can
6481    /// read it with `getenv` or `/proc/self/environ`. These fixtures are the
6482    /// output of `serde_json::to_string` on the same configs, captured from a
6483    /// build of the commit before Detcore read [`reverie::BackendCapabilities`],
6484    /// when `Config` still had one boolean field per backend switch. They are
6485    /// the variable's bytes before `dbt_detconfig_json` re-inserts the three
6486    /// deleted switches' keys; `dbt_detconfig_matches_806cf2fa_bytes` pins the
6487    /// complete variable. Every DBT run sequentializes threads, so the DBT
6488    /// cases do too. The fixtures carry one deliberate later change, the
6489    /// `fdinfo_unlisted_mount_ids` -> `mount_id_assignment_order` key rename
6490    /// described on `dbt_detconfig_tests::GOLDEN`.
6491    #[test]
6492    fn dbt_detconfig_bytes_are_unchanged_by_backend_capabilities() {
6493        let sequential = |mut config: DetConfig| {
6494            config.sequentialize_threads = true;
6495            config
6496        };
6497        let default_config = || env_free_detconfig("--epoch=2026-01-01T00:00:00Z", "--seed=0");
6498        for (name, config, before) in [
6499            (
6500                "default",
6501                default_config(),
6502                include_str!("../tests/fixtures/dbt/detconfig-before-capabilities-default.json"),
6503            ),
6504            (
6505                "dbt",
6506                prepare_backend_config(sequential(default_config()), Backend::Dbt),
6507                include_str!("../tests/fixtures/dbt/detconfig-before-capabilities-dbt.json"),
6508            ),
6509            (
6510                "rich_dbt",
6511                prepare_backend_config(sequential(rich_detconfig()), Backend::Dbt),
6512                include_str!("../tests/fixtures/dbt/detconfig-before-capabilities-rich_dbt.json"),
6513            ),
6514        ] {
6515            assert_eq!(
6516                detcore::to_legacy_backend_json(&config).unwrap(),
6517                before,
6518                "HERMIT_DBT_DETCONFIG for the {name} config changed"
6519            );
6520            // A reader of the earlier bytes recovers the capabilities.
6521            let decoded = detcore::from_legacy_backend_json(before).unwrap();
6522            assert_eq!(decoded.backend, config.backend, "{name}");
6523            assert_eq!(
6524                decoded.sequentialize_threads, config.sequentialize_threads,
6525                "{name}"
6526            );
6527            assert_eq!(
6528                detcore::to_legacy_backend_json(&decoded).unwrap(),
6529                before,
6530                "{name}"
6531            );
6532        }
6533    }
6534
6535    /// The fifteen per-backend keys, in the order the encoding has always
6536    /// carried them.
6537    const LEGACY_BACKEND_KEYS: [&str; 15] = [
6538        "cpuid_virtualized_by_backend",
6539        "backend_supports_madvise",
6540        "discover_live_file_metadata",
6541        "detect_host_clock_futex_timeouts",
6542        "syscall_clobbers_virtualized_by_backend",
6543        "cancel_killed_thread_rpcs",
6544        "backend_reports_physical_process_exits",
6545        "backend_tracks_process_children",
6546        "backend_runs_exit_robust_list",
6547        "backend_requires_thread_directed_process_signals",
6548        "backend_is_kvm",
6549        "kvm_shared_dequeue_timers",
6550        "backend_supports_parked_write_signal_interruption",
6551        "backend_virtualizes_capability_prctls",
6552        "backend_defers_vfork_child_registration",
6553    ];
6554
6555    /// Only DBT puts the encoding in a guest's environment, but every backend
6556    /// must encode the facts it had before. Each string is one digit per key
6557    /// in [`LEGACY_BACKEND_KEYS`], captured with `serde_json::to_string` from
6558    /// `prepare_backend_config` at the commit before Detcore read
6559    /// [`reverie::BackendCapabilities`], once without and once with
6560    /// sequentialized threads. LiteInst's row is its in-guest runtime, the only
6561    /// one it has. KVM's first two digits are the values
6562    /// `run_kvm` then set before it started Detcore; they are capabilities now,
6563    /// so the prepared config already has them.
6564    #[test]
6565    fn every_backend_encodes_its_previous_backend_keys() {
6566        for (backend, unsequenced, sequential) in [
6567            (Backend::Ptrace, "010000011000100", "010000011000100"),
6568            (Backend::E9patch, "010000010000100", "010000010000100"),
6569            (Backend::Liteinst, "010001010000000", "010001010000000"),
6570            (Backend::InGuestTrap, "010001010000000", "010001010000000"),
6571            (Backend::Sabre, "011111110000000", "011111110000000"),
6572            (Backend::Dbt, "010001000100000", "010001000100000"),
6573            (Backend::Kvm, "100001010010011", "100001010011011"),
6574        ] {
6575            for (sequentialize_threads, expected) in [(false, unsequenced), (true, sequential)] {
6576                let config = prepare_backend_config(
6577                    DetConfig {
6578                        sequentialize_threads,
6579                        ..DetConfig::default()
6580                    },
6581                    backend,
6582                );
6583                let encoded: serde_json::Map<String, serde_json::Value> =
6584                    serde_json::from_str(&detcore::to_legacy_backend_json(&config).unwrap())
6585                        .unwrap();
6586                let digits: String = LEGACY_BACKEND_KEYS
6587                    .iter()
6588                    .map(|key| match encoded.get(*key) {
6589                        Some(serde_json::Value::Bool(true)) => '1',
6590                        Some(serde_json::Value::Bool(false)) => '0',
6591                        other => panic!("{key} encoded as {other:?}"),
6592                    })
6593                    .collect();
6594                assert_eq!(
6595                    digits, expected,
6596                    "{backend:?}, sequentialize_threads={sequentialize_threads}"
6597                );
6598            }
6599        }
6600    }

6650    /// A launch may pass the guest a terminal when a standard descriptor is
6651    /// one, when the launcher has a controlling terminal, or when that cannot
6652    /// be read; only a launch with neither is known not to. The device number
6653    /// is read past a command name that holds spaces and parentheses.
6654    #[test]
6655    fn a_launch_may_pass_a_terminal_unless_it_proves_it_has_none() {
6656        assert!(!super::may_pass_a_terminal(false, Some(0)));
6657        assert!(super::may_pass_a_terminal(true, Some(0)));
6658        assert!(super::may_pass_a_terminal(false, Some(34817)));
6659        assert!(super::may_pass_a_terminal(false, None));
6660
6661        let stat = "4242 (a) b (c)) S 1 4242 4242 34817 4242 4194560 0 0 0 0";
6662        assert_eq!(super::parse_controlling_terminal_device(stat), Some(34817));
6663        let stat = "4242 (hermit) S 1 4242 4242 0 -1 4194560 0 0 0 0";
6664        assert_eq!(super::parse_controlling_terminal_device(stat), Some(0));
6665        assert_eq!(
6666            super::parse_controlling_terminal_device("4242 (hermit"),
6667            None
6668        );
6669        assert_eq!(
6670            super::parse_controlling_terminal_device("4242 (hermit) S 1"),
6671            None
6672        );
6673        assert!(super::controlling_terminal_device().is_some());
6674    }

6676    /// Only a backend whose blocked waits decide interruption from the kernel's
6677    /// signal state asks whether the launch may pass the guest a terminal.
6678    #[test]
6679    fn only_gated_backends_record_an_inherited_terminal() {
6680        for backend in [
6681            Backend::Dbt,
6682            Backend::Kvm,
6683            Backend::Sabre,
6684            Backend::Liteinst,
6685            Backend::InGuestTrap,
6686            Backend::E9patch,
6687        ] {
6688            let config = prepare_backend_config(super::DetConfig::default(), backend);
6689            assert!(
6690                !config.guest_may_inherit_a_terminal,
6691                "unexpected terminal record for {backend:?}"
6692            );
6693        }
6694        let config = prepare_backend_config(super::DetConfig::default(), Backend::Ptrace);
6695        assert_eq!(
6696            config.guest_may_inherit_a_terminal,
6697            super::launcher_may_pass_a_terminal()
6698        );
6699    }

6701    #[test]
6702    fn liteinst_public_dispatch_runs_in_guest_detcore() {
6703        if Backend::Liteinst.ensure_available().is_err() {
6704            return;
6705        }
6706
6707        // In-guest LiteInst has no preemption timer and refuses a maximum
6708        // timeslice, so this run disables preemption; preemption is not what
6709        // this test checks.
6710        let config = super::DetConfig {
6711            max_timeslice: None,
6712            ..super::DetConfig::default()
6713        };
6714        let mut command = super::Command::new("/bin/echo");
6715        command.arg("hello");
6716        let output = super::run_with_output_backend(
6717            command,
6718            config.clone(),
6719            false,
6720            &None,
6721            Backend::Liteinst,
6722        )
6723        .expect("run /bin/echo through in-guest LiteInst");
6724        // Include captured output so a failed backend launch reports its diagnostics.
6725        assert_eq!(
6726            output.status,
6727            super::ExitStatus::Exited(0),
6728            "guest did not exit 0.\n--- guest stderr ---\n{}\n--- guest stdout ---\n{}",
6729            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr),
6730            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout),
6731        );
6732        assert_eq!(output.stdout, b"hello\n");
6733
6734        let status = super::run_with_backend(
6735            super::Command::new("/bin/true"),
6736            config,
6737            false,
6738            &None,
6739            Backend::Liteinst,
6740        )
6741        .expect("run /bin/true through in-guest LiteInst");
6742        assert_eq!(status, super::ExitStatus::Exited(0));
6743    }

6745    #[test]
6746    #[cfg(feature = "dbt")]
6747    fn default_and_available_backends_reflect_host_probes() {
6748        assert_eq!(Backend::default(), Backend::Ptrace);
6749        let available = Backend::available().collect::<Vec<_>>();
6750        assert_eq!(
6751            available.contains(&Backend::Ptrace),
6752            Backend::Ptrace.is_available()
6753        );
6754        assert_eq!(
6755            available.contains(&Backend::Dbt),
6756            dynamorio_sdk_available() && dbt_runtime_unavailable_reason().is_none()
6757        );
6758        assert_eq!(
6759            available.contains(&Backend::Liteinst),
6760            liteinst_unavailable_reason().is_none()
6761        );
6762        // The same runtime, so available exactly when liteinst is.
6763        assert_eq!(
6764            available.contains(&Backend::InGuestTrap),
6765            available.contains(&Backend::Liteinst)
6766        );
6767        assert_eq!(
6768            available.contains(&Backend::Sabre),
6769            Backend::Sabre.is_available()
6770        );
6771        assert_eq!(
6772            available.contains(&Backend::Kvm),
6773            kvm_device_unavailable_reason(std::path::Path::new("/dev/kvm")).is_none(),
6774        );
6775        assert_eq!(
6776            available.contains(&Backend::E9patch),
6777            Backend::E9patch.is_available()
6778        );
6779    }

6830

6832            .unwrap()

6971            Some(&discovered),
6972            &executable,
6973            OsStr::new(""),
6974        )
6975        .unwrap_err();

6979    #[test]
6980    #[cfg(feature = "dbt")]
6981    fn optional_backends_report_accurate_availability() {
6982        match Backend::Dbt.ensure_available() {
6983            Ok(()) => assert!(
6984                dynamorio_sdk_available() && dbt_runtime_unavailable_reason().is_none(),
6985                "DBT reported available without its SDK and runtime"
6986            ),
6987            Err(dbt_error) => {
6988                let message = dbt_error.to_string();
6989                assert!(
6990                    message.contains("DynamoRIO runtime")
6991                        || message.contains("Detcore DBT runtime"),
6992                    "unexpected DBT availability error: {message}"
6993                );
6994            }
6995        }
6996        assert_eq!(
6997            Backend::Liteinst.ensure_available().is_ok(),
6998            liteinst_unavailable_reason().is_none()
6999        );
7000        assert_eq!(
7001            Backend::InGuestTrap.ensure_available().is_ok(),
7002            liteinst_unavailable_reason().is_none()
7003        );
7004
7005        match Backend::Kvm.ensure_available() {
7006            Ok(()) => assert!(
7007                kvm_device_unavailable_reason(std::path::Path::new("/dev/kvm")).is_none(),
7008                "KVM reported available without a usable /dev/kvm",
7009            ),
7010            Err(kvm_error) => {
7011                let message = kvm_error.to_string();
7012                assert!(
7013                    message.contains("/dev/kvm"),
7014                    "unexpected KVM availability error: {message}",
7015                );
7016                assert!(!message.contains("requires root privileges"));
7017            }
7018        }
7019    }

7044    #[test]
7045    #[cfg(feature = "dbt")]
7046    fn dbt_public_dispatch_requires_sequentialized_threads() {
7047        let command = super::Command::new("/bin/true");
7048        let config = super::DetConfig {
7049            sequentialize_threads: false,
7050            ..Default::default()
7051        };
7052
7053        let error = super::run_with_output_backend(command, config, false, &None, Backend::Dbt)
7054            .expect_err("DBT must reject non-sequentialized execution");
7055        assert!(
7056            error
7057                .to_string()
7058                .contains("dbt backend requires sequentialized threads"),
7059            "unexpected error: {error}"
7060        );
7061    }

7063    #[test]
7064    #[cfg(feature = "dbt")]
7065    fn dbt_public_dispatch_runs_echo_through_detcore() {
7066        use clap::Parser;
7067
7068        if Backend::Dbt.ensure_available().is_err() {
7069            return;
7070        }
7071
7072        let mut command = super::Command::new("/bin/echo");
7073        command.arg("hello");
7074        let mut config = super::DetConfig::parse_from(["hermit-dbt-test"]);
7075        config.sequentialize_threads = true;
7076        config.validate();
7077        let output = super::run_with_output_backend(command, config, true, &None, Backend::Dbt)
7078            .expect("run /bin/echo through DbtGuest<Detcore>");
7079
7080        // Include captured output so a failed backend launch reports its diagnostics.
7081        assert_eq!(
7082            output.status,
7083            super::ExitStatus::Exited(0),
7084            "guest did not exit 0.\n--- guest stderr ---\n{}\n--- guest stdout ---\n{}",
7085            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr),
7086            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout),
7087        );
7088        assert_eq!(output.stdout, b"hello\n");
7089        assert!(
7090            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
7091                .lines()
7092                .any(|line| line.starts_with("reverie-dbt: tool=Detcore ")),
7093            "DBT native summary did not prove Detcore dispatch: {}",
7094            String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr)
7095        );
7096    }

7144    // Keep the low-level vmcall transport covered independently from the ELF
7145    // process personality. Requires /dev/kvm; skips cleanly otherwise.
7146    #[test]
7147    fn detcore_drives_kvm_guest_for_synthetic_syscall() {
7148        use clap::Parser;
7149
7150        const MEMORY_SIZE: usize = 0x10_000;
7151        const ENTRY_POINT: u64 = 0x1000;
7152        const FRAME_ADDRESS: u64 = 0x2000;
7153
7154        let mut backend = match reverie_kvm::KvmBackend::new(MEMORY_SIZE) {
7155            Ok(backend) => backend,
7156            Err(error) => {
7157                eprintln!("skipping KVM Detcore experiment: cannot init VM: {error}");
7158                return;
7159            }
7160        };
7161
7162        // A guest that issues one `getpid` through the vmcall transport, then HLTs.
7163        backend
7164            .install_syscall(
7165                ENTRY_POINT,
7166                FRAME_ADDRESS,
7167                reverie_kvm::SyscallRequest::new(libc::SYS_getpid as u64, [0; 6]),
7168            )
7169            .expect("install synthetic getpid guest");
7170
7171        // Minimal deterministic Detcore config with RCB preemption disabled.
7172        let mut config =
7173            super::DetConfig::parse_from(["hermit-kvm-test", "--max-timeslice=disabled"]);
7174        config.validate();
7175
7176        let runtime = tokio::runtime::Builder::new_current_thread()
7177            .enable_all()
7178            .build()
7179            .expect("build tokio runtime");
7180
7181        let outcome = runtime.block_on(async {
7182            backend
7183                .run_with_tool::<super::Detcore, _>(
7184                    config,
7185                    // Executor: forward anything Detcore injects to the host.
7186                    |request: &reverie_kvm::SyscallRequest, _memory: &reverie_kvm::GuestMemory| {
7187                        // SAFETY: forwarding a register-only syscall (getpid) to the
7188                        // host; no guest pointers are dereferenced by the kernel.
7189                        unsafe {
7190                            libc::syscall(
7191                                request.number() as libc::c_long,
7192                                request.args()[0],
7193                                request.args()[1],
7194                                request.args()[2],
7195                                request.args()[3],
7196                                request.args()[4],
7197                                request.args()[5],
7198                            ) as i64
7199                        }
7200                    },
7201                )
7202                .await
7203        });
7204
7205        // The point of the experiment is to observe whether Detcore can be driven
7206        // to completion over KvmGuest at all; assert it did not error.
7207        outcome.expect("Detcore drove the synthetic KVM guest to completion");
7208    }

7210    // Minimal fake ELF payload: the loader only needs the image to NOT start
7211    // with `#!`, and a real ELF magic makes the intent obvious.
7212    const FAKE_ELF: &[u8] = b"\x7fELF\x02\x01\x01\x00 fake elf body";
7213
7214    fn shebang_tmpdir(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {
7215        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
7216            "hermit-shebang-test-{}-{}",
7217            std::process::id(),
7218            tag
7219        ));
7220        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
7221        fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
7222        dir
7223    }
7224
7225    #[test]
7226    fn resolve_shebang_plain_elf_is_unchanged() {
7227        let dir = shebang_tmpdir("plain");
7228        let prog = dir.join("prog");
7229        fs::write(&prog, FAKE_ELF).unwrap();
7230
7231        let argv = vec!["prog".to_owned(), "-a".to_owned()];
7232        let (path, out_argv, image) = resolve_kvm_shebang(&prog, argv).unwrap();
7233        assert_eq!(path, prog);
7234        assert_eq!(out_argv, vec!["prog".to_owned(), "-a".to_owned()]);
7235        assert_eq!(image, FAKE_ELF);
7236        fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
7237    }
7238
7239    #[test]
7240    fn resolve_shebang_single_level_kernel_order() {
7241        let dir = shebang_tmpdir("single");
7242        let interp = dir.join("fakebash");
7243        fs::write(&interp, FAKE_ELF).unwrap();
7244        let script = dir.join("script");
7245        // Interpreter with a single optional argument.
7246        fs::write(&script, format!("#!{} -x\necho hi\n", interp.display())).unwrap();
7247
7248        let argv = vec!["script".to_owned(), "arg1".to_owned()];
7249        let (path, out_argv, image) = resolve_kvm_shebang(&script, argv).unwrap();
7250        assert_eq!(path, interp);
7251        // Kernel order: [interp, optarg, script_path, original args after argv[0]].
7252        assert_eq!(
7253            out_argv,
7254            vec![
7255                interp.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
7256                "-x".to_owned(),
7257                script.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
7258                "arg1".to_owned(),
7259            ]
7260        );
7261        assert_eq!(image, FAKE_ELF);
7262        fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
7263    }
7264
7265    #[test]
7266    fn resolve_shebang_nested_accumulates_like_binfmt_script() {
7267        let dir = shebang_tmpdir("nested");
7268        let interp = dir.join("fakebash");
7269        fs::write(&interp, FAKE_ELF).unwrap();
7270        let mid = dir.join("mid"); // a #!-interpreter that is itself a script
7271        fs::write(&mid, format!("#!{}\n", interp.display())).unwrap();
7272        let script = dir.join("script");
7273        fs::write(&script, format!("#!{} -e\n", mid.display())).unwrap();
7274
7275        let argv = vec!["script".to_owned(), "arg1".to_owned()];
7276        let (path, out_argv, image) = resolve_kvm_shebang(&script, argv).unwrap();
7277        assert_eq!(path, interp);
7278        // Level 1: [mid, -e, script, arg1]; level 2 prepends [interp, mid].
7279        assert_eq!(
7280            out_argv,
7281            vec![
7282                interp.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
7283                mid.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
7284                "-e".to_owned(),
7285                script.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
7286                "arg1".to_owned(),
7287            ]
7288        );
7289        assert_eq!(image, FAKE_ELF);
7290        fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
7291    }

7293    #[test]
7294    fn resolve_shebang_rejects_infinite_recursion() {
7295        let dir = shebang_tmpdir("loop");
7296        let a = dir.join("a");
7297        let b = dir.join("b");
7298        fs::write(&a, format!("#!{}\n", b.display())).unwrap();
7299        fs::write(&b, format!("#!{}\n", a.display())).unwrap();
7300
7301        let argv = vec!["a".to_owned()];
7302        assert!(resolve_kvm_shebang(&a, argv).is_err());
7303        fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
7304    }
